API Monitoring Dashboard.

## 8. Partition 3 future journeys (do not activate in V1)

- Disruption detected from verified source → impact graph → options and cost → user approval → supported execution → updated trip; unsupported operations remain guidance only.
- Back-to-Home → discover current situation (with permission) → evaluate existing bookings → estimate penalties/refunds → return alternatives → user approves individual paid changes → status monitoring.
- SOS → user confirms activation as appropriate → authorized contacts/official-service guidance → verified notification delivery → incident history. Never promise a rescue team is dispatched absent contracts/integration.
- Trip completed → user selects public visibility and strips sensitive/private information → publish itinerary/photos → moderation → others browse or clone as their own draft.

### Partition 3 22 source pages/modules

1. Intelligent Trip Recovery Dashboard; 2. Alternative Search Page; 3. Recovery Cost Page; 4. Back-to-Home Dashboard; 5. Return Booking Page; 6. Safety Centre; 7. SOS Dashboard; 8. Trusted Contacts Page; 9. Advanced AI Assistant; 10. Event Discovery Page; 11. Weather Intelligence Page; 12. Group Expense Dashboard; 13. Offline Travel Page; 14. Secure Document Wallet; 15. Ghoomi Explore; 16. Travel Memories Page; 17. Community Page; 18. Public Trip Details; 19. Premium Membership Page; 20. Advanced Partner Marketplace; 21. Partner Analytics; 22. Advanced Admin Analytics.

## 9. Flow test matrix and release gates

| Test ID | Scenario | Expected result |
|---|---|---|
| `FLOW-01` | Anonymous discovery → sign-in → saved trip | Return context preserved, own trip persists |
| `FLOW-02` | New 5-day holiday → AI → edit → estimate | Valid days; correct budget; persisted revision |
| `FLOW-03` | AI unavailable | Manual plan remains useful, saves |
| `FLOW-04` | Own vehicle + own stay | Bypass checkout steps; valid trip and cost plan |
| `FLOW-05` | Supplier timeout | Honest unavailable state; other functions remain accessible |
| `FLOW-06` | Redirect to external checkout | Status stays unverified without authoritative provider proof |
| `FLOW-07` | Direct booking timeout | `PENDING/UNKNOWN`, no duplicate submission |
| `FLOW-08` | Quote amount changes | Reprice disclosed and user must approve |
| `FLOW-09` | Other account requests trip UUID | Unauthorized result; no private data leaked |
| `FLOW-10` | Mobile + keyboard account/trip flow | Accessible operations and validation feedback |
| `FLOW-11` | Support ticket creation → admin | Owner/admin see appropriate ticket; others cannot |
| `FLOW-12` | Return to saved trip after restart | Latest saved state and correct booking labels |

The master lists what the product should do; the above flows make it measurable. For visual treatment refer to `Ui/Ux design Brief.md`, for API/schema refer to `trd.md`/`Backend Schema.md`, and for implementation status refer to `tracker.md`.


========== \Backend Schema.md ==========
# GHOOMI — Backend Database Schema and Data Contracts

**Revision:** 1.1 — MySQL edition | **Current:** Partition 1 (pre-first-deployment) | **Source:** latest 53-page master PDF sections 41, 83–89, with business workflow sections 17–38. This is a **proposed MySQL 8.4 LTS implementation schema**, not a schema already deployed. It must be adapted after inspecting the actual codebase and using the user-approved MySQL database selection recorded as `ADR-001` in `memory.md`.

## 1. Modeling principles

- Relational source of truth with app-generated UUID identifiers stored as `CHAR(36)` ASCII/binary-collated primary keys, foreign keys, constraints, indexes, auditability, reversible versioned migrations, and per-user/server-side authorization.
- **Core trip records are independent of supplier inventory.** A trip can exist even when every service is in planning-only mode.
- **No fabricated financial or booking truth.** Store supplier offers as source-tagged snapshots with verification and expiry, not permanent inventory guarantees. Booking confirmation is separate from payment success and the itinerary selection.
- **Money uses BIGINT integer minor units** plus ISO 4217 currency (e.g., `INR` and paise). Don't store price in floating-point; don't apply unknown taxes or fees automatically.
- **Timestamps** use MySQL `DATETIME(6)` storing UTC by explicit backend/JDBC contract (the column does not store timezone offsets); local trip dates/times use `DATE`/`TIME` plus an IANA timezone. Configure JDBC/session timezone to UTC. Do not lose timezone at provider boundaries.
- **PII by necessity only.** Profiles/saved travelers should not include government IDs by default. Secure documents, location history and group family monitoring are deferred to later partitions.
- **Status transitions** are validated in service code and events; DB `CHECK` constraints help prevent impossible values.
- `updated_at` fields must be set by service/repository (or a reviewed trigger) consistently.

## 2. Entity relationship model

```mermaid
 erDiagram
    USERS ||--o| USER_PROFILES : has
    USERS ||--o{ SAVED_TRAVELLERS : saves
    USERS ||--o{ EMERGENCY_CONTACTS : maintains
    USERS ||--o{ TRIPS : owns
    DESTINATIONS ||--o{ PLACES : contains
    DESTINATIONS ||--o{ TRIPS : inspires
    TRIPS ||--o{ ITINERARY_DAYS : includes
    ITINERARY_DAYS ||--o{ ITINERARY_ITEMS : schedules
    PLACES ||--o{ ITINERARY_ITEMS : optional_reference
    TRIPS ||--o{ TRIP_BUDGET_ITEMS : estimates
    TRIPS ||--o{ AI_PLAN_RUNS : generates
    PROVIDERS ||--o{ PROVIDER_OFFERS : supplies
    PROVIDER_OFFERS ||--o{ TRIP_OFFER_SELECTIONS : selected_as
    TRIPS ||--o{ TRIP_OFFER_SELECTIONS : plans
    USERS ||--o{ BOOKING_INTENTS : initiates
    PROVIDERS ||--o{ BOOKING_INTENTS : processes
    BOOKING_INTENTS ||--o| BOOKINGS : results_in
    BOOKING_INTENTS ||--o{ PAYMENT_ATTEMPTS : paid_via
    BOOKINGS ||--o{ BOOKING_EVENTS : emits
    USERS ||--o{ SUPPORT_TICKETS : opens
    USERS ||--o{ USER_CONSENTS : grants
    USERS ||--o{ NOTIFICATIONS : receives
```

### 2.1 Partition 1 core tables and ownership

| ID | Table | Primary relation / purpose | PII / special rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DB-01` | `users` | Account, auth and role | Email/hash private; hashed password only |
| `DB-02` | `user_profiles` | Preferences and display name | User-scoped |
| `DB-03` | `saved_travellers` | Optional reusable travelers | Minimize age/contact details |
| `DB-04` | `emergency_contacts` | Basic traveler emergency list | Not automatically verified or alerted |
| `DB-05` | `destinations` | Curated geography and content | Licensed editorial metadata |
| `DB-06` | `places` | Attractions, food/activities | Hours/prices only when sourced |
| `DB-07` | `providers` | Supplier capabilities/contract state | Credential *references* only |
| `DB-08` | `provider_offers` | Normalized, expiring supplier snapshots | Source/time/availability status mandatory |
| `DB-09` | `trips` | Owner's complete plan summary | Row-level owner check |
| `DB-10` | `itinerary_days` | One trip's day schedule | Day unique within trip |
| `DB-11` | `itinerary_items` | Ordered itinerary components | Planned != booked |
| `DB-12` | `trip_budget_items` | Deterministic estimated line items | Paise, currency, cost-type provenance |
| `DB-13` | `trip_offer_selections` | Saved offers/own-service options | Selected != confirmed |
| `DB-14` | `ai_plan_runs` | AI orchestration status and metadata | Avoid raw secrets or excessive PII |
| `DB-15` | `booking_intents` | Checkout request and idempotency | Require provider approval |
| `DB-16` | `bookings` | Supplier-evidenced reservation status | External unverified is not confirmation |
| `DB-17` | `booking_events` | Provider booking lifecycle events | Deduped where event ID exists |
| `DB-18` | `payment_attempts` | Gateway actions with verified status | Never store card number/CVV |
| `DB-19` | `webhook_receipts` | Authenticity/deduplication/retry | Store payload hash, not secret raw body |
| `DB-20` | `notifications` | User message/delivery status | No invented provider alerts |
| `DB-21` | `support_tickets` | Customer complaint/issue record | Owner/assigned-staff only |
| `DB-22` | `safety_resources` | Official emergency directory | Source and source verification date |
| `DB-23` | `user_consents` | Terms/privacy notices and future grants | Version, purpose and withdrawal |
| `DB-24` | `audit_logs` | High-risk admin/financial/config operations | Redact sensitive payloads |

## 3. Reference MySQL 8.4 LTS DDL — Partition 1

The DDL is an **initial reference migration for a fresh MySQL 8.4 LTS database using InnoDB**. It is a specification, not evidence of an applied migration. MySQL-specific design: `CHAR(36)` UUID strings are generated by Java before insert; `JSON DEFAULT (JSON_OBJECT())` is supported using expression defaults; `DATETIME(6)` is normalized to UTC by the application; MySQL `CHECK` constraints and actual table-level foreign keys are used. `VARCHAR` is used on indexed string columns; duplicate nullable provider references use ordinary `UNIQUE` indexes because MySQL allows multiple `NULL`s. Validate this SQL against the chosen **actual MySQL server version** and an empty non-production database before deploying, then create versioned Flyway migrations. Do not implement undefined lifecycle transitions merely because a value exists.

```sql
-- Ghoomi Partition 1 reference schema (MySQL 8.4 LTS / InnoDB).
-- Generate UUIDs in Java (UUID.randomUUID().toString()); persist as CHAR(36) ASCII BINARY collation.
-- All DATETIME(6) values are UTC by application contract. Set JDBC connectionTimeZone=UTC and configure UTC session time zone.
-- Flyway should manage versioned migrations. Run only on a fresh development/staging database after review.
-- Do not disable FOREIGN_KEY_CHECKS for normal application writes.
SET time_zone = '+00:00';

CREATE TABLE users (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  email VARCHAR(254) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci NOT NULL UNIQUE,
  password_hash VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  email_verified_at DATETIME(6),
  role VARCHAR(191) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'USER' CHECK (role IN ('USER','SUPPORT','ADMIN','SUPER_ADMIN')),
  is_active TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  updated_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6)
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE user_profiles (
  user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  display_name VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,
  phone_e164 VARCHAR(20),
  travel_preferences JSON NOT NULL DEFAULT (JSON_OBJECT()),
  avatar_object_key TEXT,
  updated_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  CONSTRAINT fk_user_profiles_user_id FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE saved_travellers (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  owner_user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  display_name VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,
  age_category VARCHAR(191) CHECK (age_category IN ('CHILD','ADULT','SENIOR','UNSPECIFIED')),
  notes TEXT,
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  CONSTRAINT fk_saved_travellers_owner_user_id FOREIGN KEY (owner_user_id) REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE emergency_contacts (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  owner_user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  contact_name VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,
  contact_phone_e164 VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
  relationship_label VARCHAR(100),
  verified_at DATETIME(6),
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  CONSTRAINT fk_emergency_contacts_owner_user_id FOREIGN KEY (owner_user_id) REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE destinations (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  slug VARCHAR(160) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
  name VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  state_region VARCHAR(150),
  country_code CHAR(2) NOT NULL,
  latitude DECIMAL(9,6),
  longitude DECIMAL(9,6),
  summary TEXT,
  content_source TEXT,
  image_license_ref TEXT,
  active TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
  updated_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6)
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE places (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  destination_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  name VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  category VARCHAR(60) NOT NULL CHECK (category IN ('ATTRACTION','RESTAURANT','ACTIVITY','OTHER')),
  address_label TEXT,
  latitude DECIMAL(9,6),
  longitude DECIMAL(9,6),
  description TEXT,
  source_name VARCHAR(180),
  source_url TEXT,
  info_verified_at DATETIME(6),
  opening_hours JSON,
  estimated_cost_minor BIGINT CHECK (estimated_cost_minor >= 0),
  currency CHAR(3),
  active TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
  CONSTRAINT fk_places_destination_id FOREIGN KEY (destination_id) REFERENCES destinations(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE providers (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  code VARCHAR(80) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin NOT NULL UNIQUE,
  name VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  vertical VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL CHECK (vertical IN ('FLIGHT','TRAIN','BUS','CAB','STAY','ACTIVITY','MAP','PAYMENT')),
  capability VARCHAR(40) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PLANNING_ONLY'
    CHECK (capability IN ('PLANNING_ONLY','REDIRECT','LIVE_SEARCH_EXTERNAL_CHECKOUT','DIRECT_BOOKING')),
  authorization_status VARCHAR(25) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'UNVERIFIED'
    CHECK (authorization_status IN ('UNVERIFIED','UNDER_REVIEW','APPROVED','SUSPENDED')),
  credentials_ref VARCHAR(255), -- secret-manager reference only
  terms_url TEXT,
  enabled TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
  updated_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6)
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE provider_offers (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  provider_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  provider_offer_key VARCHAR(160) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin NOT NULL,
  vertical VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL,
  name VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  details JSON NOT NULL DEFAULT (JSON_OBJECT()),
  origin_label VARCHAR(200),
  destination_label VARCHAR(200),
  starts_at DATETIME(6),
  ends_at DATETIME(6),
  price_minor BIGINT CHECK (price_minor >= 0),
  currency CHAR(3),
  price_basis VARCHAR(24) CHECK (price_basis IN ('PER_PERSON','PER_GROUP','PER_ROOM','TOTAL','UNKNOWN')),
  data_status VARCHAR(24) NOT NULL CHECK (data_status IN ('VERIFIED_LIVE','PROVIDER_LISTED','ESTIMATED','UNAVAILABLE')),
  checked_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL,
  valid_until DATETIME(6),
  terms_url TEXT,
  redirect_url TEXT,
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  UNIQUE(provider_id, provider_offer_key, checked_at),
  CONSTRAINT fk_provider_offers_provider_id FOREIGN KEY (provider_id) REFERENCES providers(id)
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE trips (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  owner_user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  destination_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  title VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  origin_label VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  destination_label VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  start_date DATE NOT NULL,
  end_date DATE NOT NULL,
  timezone VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'Asia/Kolkata',
  traveler_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (traveler_count > 0 AND traveler_count <= 50),
  plan_mode VARCHAR(16) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'BALANCED'
    CHECK (plan_mode IN ('BUDGET','BALANCED','COMFORT','CUSTOM')),
  target_budget_minor BIGINT CHECK (target_budget_minor >= 0),
  currency CHAR(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'INR',
  own_vehicle TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
  already_have_stay TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
  preferences JSON NOT NULL DEFAULT (JSON_OBJECT()),
  status VARCHAR(35) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'DRAFT'
    CHECK (status IN ('DRAFT','PLANNED','ACTIVE','COMPLETED','CANCELLED')),
  version INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 CHECK (version >= 1),
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  updated_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  CHECK (end_date >= start_date),
  CONSTRAINT fk_trips_owner_user_id FOREIGN KEY (owner_user_id) REFERENCES users(id),
  CONSTRAINT fk_trips_destination_id FOREIGN KEY (destination_id) REFERENCES destinations(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE itinerary_days (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  trip_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  day_date DATE NOT NULL,
  day_index INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (day_index >= 1),
  title VARCHAR(255),
  UNIQUE(trip_id,day_date),
  UNIQUE(trip_id,day_index),
  CONSTRAINT fk_itinerary_days_trip_id FOREIGN KEY (trip_id) REFERENCES trips(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE itinerary_items (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  itinerary_day_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  place_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  item_order INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (item_order >= 0),
  category VARCHAR(60) NOT NULL,
  title VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  planned_start TIME,
  planned_end TIME,
  notes TEXT,
  estimate_minor BIGINT CHECK (estimate_minor >= 0),
  currency CHAR(3),
  verification_status VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'USER_PLANNED'
    CHECK (verification_status IN ('USER_PLANNED','AI_SUGGESTED','PROVIDER_LISTED','VERIFIED')),
  source_ref TEXT,
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  UNIQUE(itinerary_day_id,item_order),
  CONSTRAINT fk_itinerary_items_itinerary_day_id FOREIGN KEY (itinerary_day_id) REFERENCES itinerary_days(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  CONSTRAINT fk_itinerary_items_place_id FOREIGN KEY (place_id) REFERENCES places(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE trip_budget_items (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  trip_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  category VARCHAR(60) NOT NULL CHECK (category IN ('TRANSPORT','STAY','FOOD','LOCAL_TRAVEL','ACTIVITY','FEES','EMERGENCY_RESERVE','OTHER')),
  title VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  amount_minor BIGINT NOT NULL CHECK (amount_minor >= 0),
  currency CHAR(3) NOT NULL,
  basis VARCHAR(24) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'TOTAL' CHECK (basis IN ('TOTAL','PER_PERSON','PER_NIGHT','PER_UNIT')),
  price_status VARCHAR(28) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'ESTIMATED'
    CHECK (price_status IN ('ESTIMATED','PROVIDER_QUOTED','USER_ENTERED','PAID_VERIFIED')),
  source_ref TEXT,
  checked_at DATETIME(6),
  updated_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  CONSTRAINT fk_trip_budget_items_trip_id FOREIGN KEY (trip_id) REFERENCES trips(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE trip_offer_selections (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  trip_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  provider_offer_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  selection_kind VARCHAR(28) NOT NULL CHECK (selection_kind IN ('SUPPLIER_OFFER','OWN_VEHICLE','OWN_STAY','CUSTOM')),
  vertical VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL,
  title VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  booking_state VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PLANNED'
    CHECK (booking_state IN ('PLANNED','REDIRECTED_EXTERNAL','MANUAL_REFERENCE_UNVERIFIED','CONFIRMED','CANCELLED')),
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  CONSTRAINT fk_trip_offer_selections_trip_id FOREIGN KEY (trip_id) REFERENCES trips(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  CONSTRAINT fk_trip_offer_selections_provider_offer_id FOREIGN KEY (provider_offer_id) REFERENCES provider_offers(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE ai_plan_runs (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  trip_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  requested_by CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  run_scope VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL CHECK (run_scope IN ('WHOLE_TRIP','SINGLE_DAY')),
  model_ref VARCHAR(100),
  status VARCHAR(35) NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('PENDING','SUCCEEDED','FAILED','VALIDATION_REJECTED')),
  request_context_hash VARCHAR(128) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin,
  duration_ms INTEGER,
  error_code VARCHAR(80),
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  completed_at DATETIME(6),
  CONSTRAINT fk_ai_plan_runs_trip_id FOREIGN KEY (trip_id) REFERENCES trips(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  CONSTRAINT fk_ai_plan_runs_requested_by FOREIGN KEY (requested_by) REFERENCES users(id)
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE booking_intents (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  trip_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  provider_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  offer_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  idempotency_key VARCHAR(128) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin NOT NULL,
  capability VARCHAR(40) NOT NULL CHECK (capability IN ('REDIRECT','LIVE_SEARCH_EXTERNAL_CHECKOUT','DIRECT_BOOKING')),
  status VARCHAR(35) NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('DRAFT_INTENT','REDIRECTED_EXTERNAL','PENDING','PROCESSING','CONFIRMED','FAILED','UNKNOWN','CANCELLED')),
  quoted_amount_minor BIGINT CHECK (quoted_amount_minor >= 0),
  currency CHAR(3),
  quote_expires_at DATETIME(6),
  terms_accepted_at DATETIME(6),
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  updated_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  UNIQUE(user_id,idempotency_key),
  CONSTRAINT fk_booking_intents_user_id FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(id),
  CONSTRAINT fk_booking_intents_trip_id FOREIGN KEY (trip_id) REFERENCES trips(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  CONSTRAINT fk_booking_intents_provider_id FOREIGN KEY (provider_id) REFERENCES providers(id),
  CONSTRAINT fk_booking_intents_offer_id FOREIGN KEY (offer_id) REFERENCES provider_offers(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE bookings (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  booking_intent_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL UNIQUE,
  provider_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  trip_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  provider_booking_ref VARCHAR(180) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin,
  status VARCHAR(35) NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('PENDING','PROCESSING','CONFIRMED','FAILED','CANCELLED','UNKNOWN','COMPLETED')),
  confirmed_at DATETIME(6),
  provider_evidence_ref TEXT,
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  updated_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  CHECK (status <> 'CONFIRMED' OR (provider_booking_ref IS NOT NULL AND provider_evidence_ref IS NOT NULL)),
  CONSTRAINT fk_bookings_booking_intent_id FOREIGN KEY (booking_intent_id) REFERENCES booking_intents(id),
  CONSTRAINT fk_bookings_provider_id FOREIGN KEY (provider_id) REFERENCES providers(id),
  CONSTRAINT fk_bookings_user_id FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(id),
  CONSTRAINT fk_bookings_trip_id FOREIGN KEY (trip_id) REFERENCES trips(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE booking_events (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  booking_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  provider_event_ref VARCHAR(191) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin,
  event_type VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
  source VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
  observed_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  safe_summary JSON NOT NULL DEFAULT (JSON_OBJECT()),
  CONSTRAINT fk_booking_events_booking_id FOREIGN KEY (booking_id) REFERENCES bookings(id)
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE payment_attempts (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  booking_intent_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  gateway_code VARCHAR(60) NOT NULL,
  idempotency_key VARCHAR(128) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin NOT NULL,
  gateway_payment_ref VARCHAR(180) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin,
  amount_minor BIGINT NOT NULL CHECK (amount_minor >= 0),
  currency CHAR(3) NOT NULL,
  status VARCHAR(35) NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('NOT_STARTED','INITIATED','PENDING','SUCCEEDED','FAILED','UNKNOWN')),
  verified_at DATETIME(6),
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  UNIQUE(user_id,idempotency_key),
  CONSTRAINT fk_payment_attempts_booking_intent_id FOREIGN KEY (booking_intent_id) REFERENCES booking_intents(id),
  CONSTRAINT fk_payment_attempts_user_id FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(id)
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE webhook_receipts (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  source_code VARCHAR(60) NOT NULL,
  external_event_id VARCHAR(180) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin NOT NULL,
  payload_hash VARCHAR(128) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin NOT NULL,
  signature_valid TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
  processing_status VARCHAR(24) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'RECEIVED'
    CHECK (processing_status IN ('RECEIVED','PROCESSED','FAILED','IGNORED')),
  received_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  processed_at DATETIME(6),
  UNIQUE(source_code,external_event_id)
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE notifications (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  trip_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  category VARCHAR(60) NOT NULL,
  title VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  body TEXT NOT NULL,
  channel VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'IN_APP',
  delivery_status VARCHAR(30) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PENDING'
    CHECK (delivery_status IN ('PENDING','SENT','DELIVERED','FAILED','NOT_APPLICABLE')),
  read_at DATETIME(6),
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  CONSTRAINT fk_notifications_user_id FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  CONSTRAINT fk_notifications_trip_id FOREIGN KEY (trip_id) REFERENCES trips(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE support_tickets (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  trip_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  booking_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  assigned_staff_user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  category VARCHAR(60) NOT NULL,
  subject VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
  description TEXT NOT NULL,
  status VARCHAR(35) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'OPEN'
    CHECK (status IN ('OPEN','IN_REVIEW','WAITING_FOR_USER','RESOLVED','CLOSED')),
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  updated_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  CONSTRAINT fk_support_tickets_user_id FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(id),
  CONSTRAINT fk_support_tickets_trip_id FOREIGN KEY (trip_id) REFERENCES trips(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  CONSTRAINT fk_support_tickets_booking_id FOREIGN KEY (booking_id) REFERENCES bookings(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  CONSTRAINT fk_support_tickets_assigned_staff_user_id FOREIGN KEY (assigned_staff_user_id) REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE safety_resources (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  country_code CHAR(2) NOT NULL,
  region_label VARCHAR(191),
  resource_type VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  display_name VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,
  phone_number VARCHAR(25),
  official_url TEXT,
  source_name VARCHAR(180) NOT NULL,
  verified_at DATETIME(6),
  active TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE user_consents (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin NOT NULL,
  consent_type VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
  document_version VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
  purpose VARCHAR(200),
  granted_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  withdrawn_at DATETIME(6),
  client_context JSON NOT NULL DEFAULT (JSON_OBJECT()),
  CONSTRAINT fk_user_consents_user_id FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(id)
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE TABLE audit_logs (
  id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin PRIMARY KEY,
  actor_user_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  action VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  object_type VARCHAR(80) NOT NULL,
  object_id CHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin,
  request_id VARCHAR(120) COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_bin,
  safe_metadata JSON NOT NULL DEFAULT (JSON_OBJECT()),
  created_at DATETIME(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
  CONSTRAINT fk_audit_logs_actor_user_id FOREIGN KEY (actor_user_id) REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci;

CREATE INDEX saved_travellers_owner_idx ON saved_travellers(owner_user_id);

CREATE INDEX emergency_contacts_owner_idx ON emergency_contacts(owner_user_id);

CREATE INDEX places_destination_cat_idx ON places(destination_id,category);

CREATE INDEX provider_offers_provider_checked_idx ON provider_offers(provider_id,checked_at DESC);

CREATE INDEX trips_owner_updated_idx ON trips(owner_user_id,updated_at DESC);

CREATE INDEX trips_status_start_idx ON trips(status,start_date);

CREATE INDEX itinerary_items_day_idx ON itinerary_items(itinerary_day_id,item_order);

CREATE INDEX trip_budget_items_trip_idx ON trip_budget_items(trip_id);

CREATE INDEX trip_offer_selections_trip_idx ON trip_offer_selections(trip_id);

CREATE INDEX ai_plan_runs_trip_created_idx ON ai_plan_runs(trip_id,created_at DESC);

CREATE INDEX booking_intents_trip_idx ON booking_intents(trip_id);

-- UNIQUE indexes in MySQL permit multiple NULL values; no partial WHERE required.
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX bookings_supplier_reference_uq ON bookings(provider_id,provider_booking_ref);

CREATE INDEX bookings_user_created_idx ON bookings(user_id,created_at DESC);

CREATE INDEX booking_events_booking_idx ON booking_events(booking_id,observed_at DESC);

CREATE UNIQUE INDEX payment_gateway_ref_uq ON payment_attempts(gateway_code,gateway_payment_ref);

CREATE INDEX notifications_user_created_idx ON notifications(user_id,created_at DESC);

CREATE INDEX support_tickets_owner_status_idx ON support_tickets(user_id,status);

CREATE INDEX user_consents_type_idx ON user_consents(user_id,consent_type,granted_at DESC);

CREATE INDEX audit_logs_object_idx ON audit_logs(object_type,object_id,created_at DESC);
```

### 3.1 Migration hardening required before real production

- Trip date `itinerary_days.day_date` must be checked against its parent trip dates **in service logic and integration tests** (cross-table constraints require triggers or guarded writes).
- Booking `CONFIRMED` requires valid supplier evidence, not just non-null text; enforce this in a verified adapter/event service, never let a client set that status.
- Booking/provider/user/trip foreign keys must be **cross-checked for same owner/provider** in transaction service; FK existence alone does not establish authorized relationships.
- `provider_offers` cache rows need a cleanup/retention job and the search process must not reuse expired inventory as live. Provider terms/rights decide permitted caching.
- `places`/`destinations` editorial data requires source/licensing/attribution and periodic review; empty `info_verified_at` means no live claim.
- Login sessions/tokens and password recovery tables depend on the selected session mechanism; **do not add authentication storage with conflicting security assumptions** until auth design approved.
- Implement proper state-transition services, optimistic locking on trip version and audit logs for sensitive changes.
- Review `ON DELETE` rules for financial records and applicable retention obligations. Retained payment/booking records should not be cascade-deleted by user deletion; use compliant pseudonymization/erasure workflows.
- Add appropriate constraints on text length, currencies, phone normalization, time order and relationship consistency at service/DB boundary; test invalid and boundary cases.

### 3.2 JDBC/JPA mapping note (MySQL)

Map Java UUIDs explicitly to the string `CHAR(36)` representation (for Hibernate 6, an explicit VARCHAR JDBC type strategy such as `@JdbcTypeCode(SqlTypes.VARCHAR)` or equivalent tested converter may be needed; do not assume Hibernate's default UUID binding uses the same physical representation). Ensure application-level UTC normalization; use binary collation for machine identifiers and test exact-case idempotency matching. Check JSON default expressions on the deployed server version.

## 4. Normalized data behavior and invariants

### `DB-AUTH`: Users and traveler ownership

- Every saved traveler, emergency contact, profile, trip, support ticket and notification has an owner or explicit staff permission. User B cannot fetch user A's rows even by supplying IDs.
- Sensitive contact details encrypted at rest as infrastructure supports, omitted from logs and analytics.
- Admin role is server-side; never use a client-provided `role` claim as the sole privilege source.

### `DB-TRIP`: Trip, version and itinerary

- `trips.version` increments on saved mutation. `PATCH /trips/{id}` verifies `If-Match`/version to prevent lost edits.
- Day indexes are contiguous and unique per trip; activities ordered by `item_order`; reordering should be transactional to avoid unique-index collisions.
- An itinerary item is *planned*, not automatically a supplier booking. Linking to `places` or `provider_offers` is optional.
- Own vehicle and existing accommodation are planning choices, with estimated costs if user supplies them. Do not create fake reservation records.

### `DB-BUDGET`: Money and provenance

- `trip_budget_items.amount_minor` is nonnegative; `sum` over enabled categories produces estimated total. If discounts require signed adjustments, model them explicitly and preserve source/eligibility.
- `price_status`: `ESTIMATED`, `PROVIDER_QUOTED`, `USER_ENTERED`, `PAID_VERIFIED`. A quoted amount expiring must be marked stale; paid only from real verified transaction.
- No cross-currency addition without explicit user-confirmed FX method and valid rate/time. V1 can restrict budgeting to INR for simplicity if agreed.
- Budget remaining = target budget - latest valid estimated total, and may be negative. Do not silently floor at zero.

### `DB-OFFER`: Supplier truth and capability

- Provider capability cannot exceed approved contract/technical rights; keep `enabled=false` until tested and authorized.
- `checked_at`, `valid_until`, `data_status`, provider, price basis and terms must travel through APIs to UI.
- Cache is **not** a source of perpetual availability. `ESTIMATED` cannot be treated as a direct-bookable live quote.

### `DB-BOOK`: Idempotency and evidence

- Unique `(user_id, idempotency_key)` for booking/payment action, and unique supplier event refs where provided.
- A redirect or manual external reference stays unverified. Provider confirmation creates/updates `bookings` only after authenticated evidence.
- Payment and booking states transition independently; unknown outcome stays pending until reconciled. Do not reissue a financial action based only on client retry.
- Lock/revalidate quote and user acceptance before direct booking. Store audit record for consent and accepted price/terms where required.

### `DB-OPS`: Support, notifications and safety

- Ticket scope includes owner and assigned staff; attached booking/trip must belong to same owner unless authorized admin case.
- Notification `SENT/DELIVERED` is based on actual dispatch results, not simply queue creation.
- Emergency contacts in V1 **are not automatically alerted**. Advanced sharing/SOS data belongs to later partitions with explicit consent.

## 5. Expected API ↔ entity mapping

| Endpoint family | Entity group | Authorization/control |
|---|---|---|
| `/auth/*`, `/me/*` | users, profiles, travelers | Session, verification, object ownership |
| `/destinations`, `/places` | destinations, places | Public read, admin-only content write |
| `/trips/*` | trips, itinerary days/items, budgets, selections | Owner with version checks |
| `/trips/*/plans/generate` | ai_plan_runs and itinerary | Owner; rate/cost quotas and schema validator |
| `/search/*` | providers, provider_offers | Provider capability + response source; rate limits |
| `/booking-intents`, `/bookings` | booking_intents, bookings, events | Owner, supplier mode, idempotency and evidence |
| `/payments/*`, `/webhooks/*` | payment_attempts, webhook_receipts | Gateway verification, no client trust |
| `/support/*`, `/notifications` | support_tickets, notifications | Owner/assigned staff |
| `/admin/*` | providers, content, tickets, audit | Privileged RBAC with audit |

## 6. Partition 2 schema extensions — defer migrations until approved

| New table(s) | Purpose and key requirements |
|---|---|
| `trip_members`, `group_invites` | Organizer/member roles, invite expiry, selected visibility |
| `location_sharing_grants`, `location_events` | Recipient scope, explicit consent, expiry, revocation, location timestamp, privacy retention |
| `trip_events` | Verified source of delays/arrival/progress, freshness and traceability |
| `booking_modification_requests`, `cancellation_requests`, `refunds` | User approval, provider policy/fees, confirmed states and reconciliation |
| `partner_accounts`, `partner_inventory`, `settlements`, `commissions` | Authorized business roles, listing owner, commercial records |
| `documents` | Private bucket key, MIME/size/hash/scan/retention, uploader/access audits |
| `notification_preferences`, `notification_deliveries` | Channel opt-in, attempts, delivery statuses |
| `support_messages`, `support_escalations` | Ticket threads, assignees and retention |

### 6.1 Future consent model

`location_sharing_grants(grant_id, traveler_user_id, recipient_user_id/contact_id, purpose, scope, start_at, expires_at, revoked_at, created_at)` plus a narrowly scoped location point store. Location use is impossible before grant validation. A guardian relationship alone is **not** permission; browser GPS must be allowed independently. Background location may not work reliably in web contexts.

## 7. Partition 3 schema extensions — defer until validated

| Future entities | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `disruptions`, `trip_impact_edges`, `recovery_options`, `recovery_approvals`, `recovery_actions` | Detect verified issue, proposed options, cost, consent and auditable changes |
| `return_plans`, `return_service_options`, `return_approvals` | Back-to-Home decisions, approvals, provider outcomes |
| `sos_incidents`, `sos_notifications` | Delivery status and incident support; never imply external dispatch without agreement |
| `expense_groups`, `expense_lines`, `member_shares`, `settlements` | Group expenses and calculated balances |
| `offline_sync_snapshots` | User-authorized cached snapshot, last sync and expiry |
| `document_wallet_items`, `document_access_logs` | Sensitive documents with retention/deletion and access auditing |
| `public_trips`, `trip_media`, `public_reviews`, `moderation_cases` | Explicit publication opt-in, privacy-safe snapshots, content moderation |
| `membership_plans`, `subscriptions`, `entitlements` | Premium benefits and verified payment status |
| `partner_marketplace_listings`, `promotions` | Authorized partner service catalog and disclosures |

## 8. Database indexes, tenancy and audit

- High-priority indexes: trips `(owner_user_id, updated_at)`, itinerary items by day/order, places `(destination_id, category)`, offers `(provider_id, checked_at)`, bookings `(user_id, created_at)`, support tickets `(user_id,status)`, notifications `(user_id,created_at)`.
- Test explain plans under representative data, not speculative premature indexing.
- Backend authorization must enforce ownership for every nested trip item; MySQL does not offer PostgreSQL-style native RLS; enforce tenant ownership in every Spring Boot service/repository query, with permission tests and least-privilege DB credentials.
- Audit events: staff access, provider config, booking/payment transitions, consent changes, sensitive file access (future), privileged deletion and content publication.
- Retention/erasure: align business/compliance needs with data minimization; no blanket cascade removal of auditable financial events.

## 9. Migration/test plan and definition of done

1. Inventory existing DB/schema/migrations; document conflicts before editing.
2. MySQL is approved in `ADR-001`. Inspect existing code/ORM before making disruptive changes; create reviewed, versioned Flyway migrations in dependency order for MySQL 8.4 LTS.
3. Run clean-schema creation and migration forward tests with representative non-production fixtures.
4. Add FK/uniqueness/check/ownership tests, especially cross-account direct-object reference tests.
5. Test concurrent trip version updates, duplicate idempotency key, duplicated webhook, stale offer and unknown payment outcomes.
6. Validate backup restore and schema rollback/forward recovery in staging; never test destructive recovery against production.
7. Update OpenAPI/DTO mappings, `trd.md`, `tracker.md` and decision log when schema changes.

The master source defines the feature inventory and relational concepts; the MySQL-specific concrete DDL and naming above are a starting proposal that must be reviewed before real migration execution. A separate SQL reference copy is included in `database/mysql/V1__ghoomi_partition1_reference.sql` (copy into the backend Flyway migrations directory only after repository audit).


========== \Implementation plan.md ==========
# GHOOMI — Implementation Plan and Release Gates

**Revision:** 1.0 | **Active scope:** Partition 1 (before first deployment) | **Status:** plan only; not work completed | **Source:** 53-page master sections 40–43, 58–60, 79–81 and 82–94. Task IDs, ordering, evidence gates and time ranges are proposed to support agent execution.

## 1. Delivery strategy

Create Ghoomi **as a usable vertical-slice product**, not 55 disconnected static pages. First release provides a reliable travel search/comparison and complete-trip AI planning experience. Every route must have useful function or honest unavailable state. Actual supplier bookings and Ghoomi payment handling require approved integration rights; supplier modes must reflect actual capabilities.

### 1.1 Release language

- **Build target / Partition 1:** accounts, search/discovery (flight, train, bus, cab, stay), holiday wizard, AI editable itinerary, budget, trip hub, food/activity discovery, basic booking handoff or authorized direct flow, safety directory, notifications, support/admin.
- **After deployment / Partition 2:** deeper bookings/payment/refunds, group coordination, consent-based family monitoring/live status, private documents, stronger partner/support operations.
- **Long-term / Partition 3:** intelligent trip recovery, Back-to-Home, SOS system, advanced context-aware AI, public travel/social and marketplace, premium/offline/financial extensions.

**Important:** Partition 1 has P0 and P1 sub-priorities; P1 features still belong to the first-deployment product plan, but provider-dependent portions can only be active to an authorized/truthful depth. Never classify a stubbed checkout as completed.

## 2. Prerequisite dependency graph

```mermaid
flowchart TD
 A[IMP-00 Repo and provider audit] --> B[IMP-01 Architecture / environments / CI]
 B --> C[IMP-02 Auth / users / DB]
 C --> D[IMP-03 Trip wizard + persistence]
 B --> E[IMP-04 Destination catalog + UI shell]
 B --> F[IMP-05 Provider capability registry]
 F --> G[IMP-06 Travel search verticals]
 C --> H[IMP-07 AI itinerary + validation]
 D --> H
 E --> H
 H --> I[IMP-08 Editor + deterministic budget]
 G --> I
 I --> J[IMP-09 Unified trip hub]
 G --> K[IMP-10 Booking / redirect + verified state]
 C --> K
 J --> L[IMP-11 Safety/support/admin/notifications]
 K --> M[IMP-12 E2E, security, performance, legal readiness]
 L --> M
 M --> N[IMP-13 Staging → controlled release]
```

**Parallelization:** after architecture/schema/API contracts stabilize, frontend page families and backend modules can be developed concurrently with typed fixtures. Test fixtures are non-production; contract tests govern real backend integration.

## 3. Execution phases — Partition 1 (ACTIVE)

### `IMP-00` — Repository discovery, requirements and external-dependency audit

**Input:** `AGENTS.md`, `rules.md`, all nine specs, master PDF and current codebase.

- [ ] Inventory frameworks/package versions, existing routes, APIs, migrations, services, tests, deployment and secrets/config **names only**.
- [ ] Identify existing working features vs visual mockups and document actual evidence.
- [ ] Confirm tech approach (`ADR-001`); do not replace sound existing stack without approval.
- [ ] Build provider authorization matrix: flight, train, bus, cab, hotel, maps/places, AI, payment, SMS/email; record `PLANNING_ONLY` until evidence permits a stronger mode.
- [ ] Confirm launch geography, initial supported destinations, content image licensing and support operator path.
- [ ] Turn the PRD requirements into a small prioritized feature backlog with clear acceptance tests.
- [ ] Establish a privacy/legal review checklist for India's applicable travel, consumer, privacy, tax and payment obligations.

**Exit gate:** honest current-state report, proposed architecture decision, top risks and an approved first vertical slice. **Never** claim code is implemented because a specification exists.

### `IMP-01` — Foundation: source control, API shell, migrations, design system and CI

**Requirements:** `GHO-P1-QUALITY`, `HOME`; `API` conventions; `DB` baseline.

- [ ] Create or align frontend/backend repo structure without destructive replacement.
- [ ] Establish Tailwind/theming tokens or equivalent existing styles; build accessible components (forms, buttons, alerts, cards, nav, tabs, skeletons).
- [ ] Build responsive shell, landing structure, public routes and guarded authenticated layout.
- [ ] Set up user-approved MySQL 8.4 LTS/InnoDB + reviewed Flyway migrations; ensure isolated dev/test/staging DB. Convert existing schema only after repository audit; do not silently wipe data.
- [ ] Implement standard API error responses, request IDs, structured logging and safe health checks.
- [ ] Configure CI build/lint/types, unit/integration test runner, dependency scanning and `.env.example` variable names.
- [ ] Create staging deployment baseline and non-production test data policy.

**Exit gate:** frontend and backend build; core route renders at mobile/desktop; DB migration + rollback/restore strategy reviewed; test suite runs; secrets absent from client bundle.

### `IMP-02` — Secure accounts, profile, saved travelers (`AUTH`, `PROFILE`)

- [ ] Signup/login/logout, email verification and recovery with secure sessions and rate limits.
- [ ] Profile edit, saved traveler creation/edit/delete, optional emergency contacts (safety module completes later).
- [ ] Permission middleware and security tests for every owner-scope resource.
- [ ] Route guards and return-to-action after signup/login; accessible account forms and error states.
- [ ] Record acceptance of current policy versions where applicable; never collect IDs not required for real booking.

**Exit gate:** two separate test users cannot access each other's profile/trips/travelers; session expiry, account recovery and brute-force rate limits pass; DB persistence verified.

### `IMP-03` — Trip creation and saved drafts (`TRIP`)

- [ ] Implement trip wizard route/date/traveler/budget/preferences DTOs and server validation.
- [ ] Add `Own Vehicle` and `Already Have Stay`; preserve draft without supplier selection.
- [ ] Save, resume, edit, delete/cancel as allowed; version conflicts and ownership checks.
- [ ] Create initial itinerary day scaffolding from date range; store local timezone.
- [ ] My Trips list with draft/planned status and clear continue action.

**Exit gate:** user builds a trip, logs out, returns and sees exact latest owned saved state; invalid dates/counts rejected; unauthorized access rejected.

### `IMP-04` — Destination/food/activity catalog and responsive discovery (`DEST`, `FOOD`, `ACT`)

- [ ] Identify authorized editorial/map/place data sources and image licenses. Publish a narrow verified or clearly curated destination set first.
- [ ] Build destination search, listing, detail and relevant place categories; appropriate filtering/empty states.
- [ ] Add restaurants/attractions/activities to an existing itinerary as **planned items**.
- [ ] Expose sources and freshness; unknown opening times/prices stay unknown.
- [ ] Set admin-controlled editorial updates and input validation.

**Exit gate:** visitors explore meaningful destinations; users save places to a trip; nothing is falsely described as open/current/booked.

### `IMP-05` — Partner adapter platform and capability gates (`PARTNER`)

- [ ] Implement provider registry/health feature flags and four capability modes.
- [ ] Define normalized offer DTO, pricing/basis/freshness, verified source and supplier errors.
- [ ] Add backend adapter interface for `search`, `details`, `quote/reprice`, `handoff`, and later `book` only when authorized.
- [ ] Implement timeouts, rate limiting, retries/circuit breaker, cache TTL and error metrics.
- [ ] Record approved provider access, license terms, sandbox test credentials and launch mode **without placing secrets in documents**.
- [ ] Provide deterministic contract fixtures exclusively in test/dev; deny synthetic production inventory.

**Exit gate:** unapproved provider cannot serve bookable offers; each adapter has contract test and failure isolation; source/status visible to frontend.

### `IMP-06` — Search verticals and comparison (`TRANSPORT`, `STAY`)

Incrementally build shared search/results/detail components then each vertical:

- [ ] Flights: route, date, passengers; results, filters, details, baggage/terms where supplied.
- [ ] Trains: source/destination stations, date, classes, provider rights; avoid unauthorized ticketing claims.
- [ ] Buses: operator, boarding/drop points, seat category and genuine supplier details.
- [ ] Cabs: pickup/drop, local/airport/outstation, quote estimate versus supplier-confirmed label.
- [ ] Own-vehicle route planning and optional user-estimated costs, no booking CTA.
- [ ] Hotels/stays: date/guest/room search, compare, detail and room selection only when source supports it; own stay option.
- [ ] Shared no-results, stale-quote, supplier unavailable, rate-limit and redirect states.
- [ ] All sorting/filters use real data and preserve criteria while navigating.

**Exit gate:** every enabled search category has an honest, functional discovery path and next action. At least one chosen pilot provider category should achieve a tested approved redirect/live search path before claiming a booking-oriented public launch; if none is authorized, limit launch messaging to **travel planning/discovery beta**, not a full booking platform.

### `IMP-07` — AI planning integration and manual fallback (`AI`)

- [ ] Configure approved AI provider (if credentials/terms available) behind backend and request quotas.
- [ ] Create structured itinerary generation DTO and schema validators for dates, activities, time windows, provenance and user constraints.
- [ ] Implement Budget/Balanced/Comfort/Custom preference interpretation and source-grounded suggestions.
- [ ] Add AI-run status/logging (no sensitive raw prompts); errors and retries safely bounded.
- [ ] Implement manual plan creation from empty schedule and optional deterministic template if AI fails.
- [ ] UI shows AI suggestions as editable/not booked and supports regeneration with clear scope.

**Exit gate:** test five-day itinerary includes only intended dates and can be edited/saved; AI errors never block manual trip planning; no artificial live prices or bookings generated.

### `IMP-08` — Itinerary editing and deterministic budget (`EDIT`, `BUDGET`)

- [ ] Add/edit/remove/reorder itinerary items transactionally with concurrency control and accessible controls.
- [ ] Build cost line-item service in integer paise; categories, per-person/total units and source/estimate statuses.
- [ ] Calculate target minus estimates, flags for over-budget and optional emergency reserve.
- [ ] Compare planning modes and category-level budget/quality overrides.
- [ ] Recalculate on traveler, date, offer or activity changes; show stale supplier quotes.
- [ ] Unit-test overflow/currency mismatch/zero/negative/rounding and client/server consistency.

**Exit gate:** editing a plan persists and produces reproducible deterministic correct totals; AI cannot silently change booked services or override cost math.

### `IMP-09` — Unified dashboard and lifecycle (`HUB`)

- [ ] Build My Trips, Trip Hub, itinerary/timeline, budget, selected services, booking/reference/safety panels.
- [ ] Show selected-but-unbooked versus supplier-confirmed or unverified external references.
- [ ] Load/refresh data from server after mutation and maintain clean empty/loading/error states.
- [ ] Support simple `DRAFT → PLANNED → ACTIVE → COMPLETED` lifecycle with explicit transitions; no live GPS implication.
- [ ] Review mobile and accessibility states for a large itinerary.

**Exit gate:** one saved trip provides accurate consistent view across modules and repeat sessions, with no false booking or tracking claims.

### `IMP-10` — Booking handoffs and payment status (`BOOK`, `PAY`)

**Dependencies:** real partner capability/approval and operational readiness. Build UI/contract foundation regardless, but **do not enable chargeable APIs** until integration approvals and tests exist.

- [ ] Design quote/reprice, terms, provider-aware handoff and booking intent state.
- [ ] Approved redirects: open correct provider checkout and clearly state who completes booking; keep external status unverified.
- [ ] Approved direct mode only: verify quote/availability, user accepts final fees/policy, submit idempotent provider request, await verified supplier confirmation.
- [ ] Payment where configured: secured gateway, verified callbacks, independent payment vs booking status, audit and unknown-timeout reconciliation.
- [ ] Support `pending`, `unknown`, `failed` and `confirmed` UX with actual source/reference; no fabricated success state.
- [ ] Add duplicate-action, webhook replay, quote change, payment/booking mismatch and provider downtime tests.

**Exit gate:** no internal `CONFIRMED` record without authoritative supplier evidence; no `SUCCEEDED` payment without verified gateway evidence; redirects remain labeled external; financial operations are auditable and recoverable.

### `IMP-11` — First-release supporting operations (`SAFETY`, `NOTIFY`, `SUPPORT`, `ADMIN`)

- [ ] Emergency contacts, accurate official emergency information and basic user guidance; no automated SOS/location tracking claims.
- [ ] Notification records/in-app views plus configured email/SMS delivery where real provider exists, with failure status.
- [ ] Support ticket create/read/status with trip/booking links and owner/assigned-staff authorization.
- [ ] Admin overview: role-gated users/trips/content/provider state/support tickets and relevant audit trails.
- [ ] Create operational runbook for provider outage, booking/payment uncertainty, urgent support, data incident.

**Exit gate:** user finds help and support, operations staff see only authorized data, important actions are audited, dispatch/notification success is never fabricated.

### `IMP-12` — Pre-launch quality and compliance verification (`QUALITY`)

**Blockers must remain visible in `tracker.md`.**

- [ ] Validate all 17 first-deployment source criteria (master section 43) and PRD acceptance criteria.
- [ ] Run core journey E2E: auth → trip → AI/manual → search → edit → budget → save → reopen → supported handoff / honest unavailable.
- [ ] Verify source-only live claims, auth isolation, role permissions, quote reprice, financial state/idempotency if enabled.
- [ ] Test mobile/desktop and keyboard accessible interactions and loading/empty/error states.
- [ ] Run unit, integration, provider sandbox, security, dependency and regression checks with actual results.
- [ ] Complete privacy/cookie/terms/refund/support disclosure review aligned to actual commercial model; obtain qualified legal review before commercial launch.
- [ ] Test database backup restoration and staging rollback, observe alerts and check on-call/support readiness.

**Exit gate:** written signoff for release readiness; no critical unresolved auth, payment, booking-truth, privacy or source-provenance issues.

### `IMP-13` — Controlled first deployment and follow-up

- [ ] Prepare version tag, migration plan, environment-specific credentials, DNS/TLS, provider feature flags, monitoring and rollback steps.
- [ ] Deploy staging; run smoke tests and approved provider sandbox validations. Release production only after gates.
- [ ] Public messaging matches actual integration modes. If only planning/discovery is validated, call the release **planning/discovery beta**, not an end-to-end direct booking system.
- [ ] Set up customer support intake, incident escalation and privacy-safe analytics.
- [ ] Observe real user journeys; capture bug list and prioritize fixes before Partition 2.

**Exit gate:** measured production smoke test, incident route, feature flags, rollback runbook and documented post-launch metrics. Do not report deployed until deployment succeeded.

## 4. Intended parallel workstreams and ownership (roles, not named people)

| Workstream | Responsibilities | Hand-off contract |
|---|---|---|
| Product/UX | PRD and source alignment, screens, accessibility, microcopy | Requirement IDs, flows, reviewed states |
| Frontend | UI components, routes, forms, states, integration with API | Typed DTOs and responsive acceptance checks |
| Backend/data | Auth, trips, budget, provider gateway, booking status, schema | OpenAPI, migrations and tests |
| AI/integration | Provider mode registry, AI validation, supplier adapters | Source provenance, quotas, failure tests |
| QA/security | Functional, E2E, accessibility, auth-negative tests | Evidence and blocker list |
| DevOps/operations | CI/staging/prod, secrets, backups, monitoring, rollback | Deployment and recovery evidence |

In a small team, one person may cover multiple roles. Parallel work begins after agreement on core DTOs and user flows.

## 5. Suggested milestones (illustrative, not commitments)

| Milestone | Outcome | Dependency |
|---|---|---|
| M0 | Audit, scope, tech and provider capability map approved | None |
| M1 | Foundation/auth/DB/staging and landing functional | M0 |
| M2 | Trip wizard, persistence, destination catalog | M1 |
| M3 | AI manual fallback, itinerary, budget, trip hub | M2 |
| M4 | Core search verticals/authorized partner handoffs | M1/M2 and supplier access |
| M5 | Basic safety/support/admin/notification | M1–M4 |
| M6 | Release hardening, operator readiness, compliant launch | All applicable M0–M5 |

**Scheduling:** choose realistic sprint capacity after repo/team/partner audit. Provider authorization and commercial approvals can dominate elapsed time; time estimates without access information are unreliable.

## 6. Source page inventory coverage

The source enumerates **55 initial page/module entries** grouped into foundation (7), trip/AI (10), transport (14), accommodation (7), food/activities (6), booking/operations (11). `App Flow.md` preserves exact numbering and maps entries to reusable route families. ## 7. Explicit post-launch roadmap — not active tasks

### Partition 2 — operational expansion

1. Expand approved flight/train/bus/cab/hotel integrations and accurate booking state synchronization.
2. Add advanced payment recording, gateway events, reconciliation, partner settlements, commissions and supported cancellation/refund flows with explicit consent.
3. Introduce group organizer/member roles, invitations, shared itineraries and per-member permissions.
4. Enable opt-in family monitoring only after explicit consent, location-sharing duration/revocation, recipient verification and realistic browser/OS constraints.
5. Add source-backed live trip events, notifications, protected booking-document storage and advanced support.
6. Build partner self-service and platform operations dashboards once inventory ownership/contractual processes exist.

**Partition 2 release gate:** full financial/source integrity, support escalation, privacy/consent evidence, webhook/idempotency/reconciliation tests and feature flags.

### Partition 3 — intelligent ecosystem

1. Advanced AI travel assistant and current verified event/weather advice.
2. Intelligent trip recovery (source-confirmed disruption → impacted services → alternatives → cost differences → user approval → supported execution).
3. Back-to-Home with user-approved return options, no automatic cancellation/charges.
4. Advanced SOS/trusted-contact coordination with explicit delivery status and no false emergency dispatch claims.
5. Group expense splitting, limited offline essentials, sensitive document wallet subject to review.
6. Opt-in public trips, memories, Ghoomi Explore, moderation, partner marketplace, premiums and advanced analytics.

**Partition 3 release gate:** operational maturity, necessary real provider/safety partnerships, legal/data retention review and validated demand. Do not introduce complexity based only on the number of future feature names.

## 8. Quality gates and source-backed first deployment checklist

| Gate ID | Verify |
|---|---|
| `REL-01` | Account registration/login actually work |
| `REL-02` | Trips create/save/reopen correctly |
| `REL-03` | Preferences are stored and applied |
| `REL-04` | AI itinerary or honest manual fallback is useful |
| `REL-05` | Budget arithmetic is correct |
| `REL-06` | Supported transport options can be compared |
| `REL-07` | Supported accommodation can be discovered |
| `REL-08` | Restaurants and activities can be explored |
| `REL-09` | Trips can be customized and changes persist |
| `REL-10` | Unified dashboard shows accurate data |
| `REL-11` | Every enabled booking integration operates as authorized/claimed |
| `REL-12` | Payment state is accurate where payment is integrated |
| `REL-13` | Admin operations are secure and functional |
| `REL-14` | API/provider failures are handled without fake success |
| `REL-15` | Security/privacy and compliance steps are met |
| `REL-16` | Desktop/mobile flows pass functional/accessibility smoke tests |
| `REL-17` | End-to-end core user workflows pass testing |

**Additional safety gates:** backup restore exercised; rollback rehearsed; source/availability/freshness labels correct; zero verified critical cross-account or financial vulnerability; support owner identified; actual partner rights recorded.

========== \memory.md ==========
# GHOOMI — Persistent project memory and decision log

**Role:** durable project-context handoff for coding agents across sessions, *not* a substitute for source control, deployment logs or user approval. **Created:** 2026-11-03. **Stage:** specification only; all software/partner integrations are unverified until inspected.

## 1. Identity and objective

- **Name:** Ghoomi.
- **Category:** smart travel planning, discovery, authorized booking and trip-management platform.
- **Brand statement:** “You Enjoy the Journey. Ghoomi Manages the Journey.”
- **Vision:** “From Home to Home.”
- **Initial positioning:** familiar MakeMyTrip-like transport/hotel/holiday search and comparison, plus AI-generated and editable itineraries, transparent budgets and a centralized trip dashboard **in version 1**.
- **Long-term objective:** help travelers coordinate an entire journey, not merely complete individual service bookings.

## 2. Scope is partitioned, not a collection of separate products

| Partition | When | Approved product direction |
|---|---|---|
| **1 — Initial release** | Before first public deployment | Working accounts, destination/trip/AI/budget/discovery/hub, basic booking pathways and payment status *only if authorized*, basic safety information, support/admin foundation |
| **2 — Operational expansion** | After deployment | Expanded bookings/payment/refund operations, collaboration, consent-based family tracking, live updates, documents, richer notification/support/partner modules |
| **3 — Ecosystem** | Long term | Intelligent recovery, Back-to-Home, advanced SOS, contextual AI, weather/events, expense split, offline, public trips, community, marketplace, premium |

**Never mistake a provider-dependent desired feature for an implemented integration.** `PLANNING_ONLY` is a legitimate V1 mode where live booking is not yet authorized, with accurate labeling and a real next action such as manual planning; do not market it as bookable.

## 3. Stable product decisions

- Users control plan changes, budget trade-offs and paid actions; AI recommends but does not book independently.
- Optional **Own Vehicle** and **Already Have Accommodation** paths avoid unwanted booking steps.
- Trip planner accepts origin, destination, dates, traveler count, budget, pace/comfort, modes, stay/food/activity preferences; output is day-wise, editable and persisted.
- Budget arithmetic is deterministic. AI may recommend savings but cannot authoritatively calculate payment totals or invent offers. Store monetary amounts as integer paise with ISO currency.
- Results and quotes must show data provenance, timestamp, status, taxes/fees, cancellation conditions where applicable and the actual next-action mode.
- Provider booking confirmation and payment-provider confirmation are distinct authoritative events. No false confirmed states.
- Safety starts with emergency-contact storage and relevant emergency-service information; continuous live location/family monitoring and automated SOS are **not** V1 requirements.
- Mobile-first responsive experience; accessible keyboard navigation, form errors and zero dead-end clickable controls.
- User security, role enforcement, privacy, backups and error handling exist from the first release.

## 4. Proposed engineering baseline — not in original source as a final selection

The 53-page master lists **React/Next.js**, **Java Spring Boot**, **MySQL/PostgreSQL**, **Spring Security/JWT**, official provider APIs, an AI API, maps and payment integrations as options. This pack proposes a concrete default for a **new, empty** repository: **Next.js (React + TypeScript + Tailwind) frontend; Java Spring Boot modular-monolith REST API; MySQL 8.4 LTS/InnoDB (user-selected); Flyway migrations; Spring Security server-managed HTTP-only sessions or carefully managed token auth; secure object storage if required.** Do not replatform an existing project without a specific approved migration decision. This is recorded in `ADR-001`: **the database is approved as MySQL**; the remaining stack choices are still proposed pending repository inspection.

### Database decision update — 2026-11-03

The user explicitly changed the database from PostgreSQL to **MySQL**. Treat this as a binding project requirement for new work. Use **MySQL 8.4 LTS + InnoDB** as the proposed server baseline (actual provider/version remains to be verified), `CHAR(36)` UUID strings generated application-side, UTC `DATETIME(6)`, MySQL `JSON`, `CHECK`, foreign keys, and versioned Flyway migrations. Do **not** claim migrations were executed or existing project data converted; audit the real repository and any existing database first.

## 5. Source hierarchy and interpretation

1. **Latest primary source:** `reference/Ghoomi_Working_Platform_Master.pdf`, 53 pages, *COMPLETE PRODUCT VISION, FUNCTIONAL REQUIREMENTS & THREE-PART DEVELOPMENT ROADMAP*. It defines 95 topical sections and 55 initial-release page/module entries; those entries need not all become separate URL routes.
2. **Translated implementation specs:** nine Markdown files at project root plus `AGENTS.md` and `README.md`. They add IDs, testable acceptance criteria, recommended architecture and safeguards; these additions are clearly **proposals** unless approved/implemented.
3. **Prior concept documents:** broader context for future Ghoomi ambition; do not silently pull long-term features into Partition 1.
4. **Reality:** inspected code, real partner permissions, signed agreements, running tests, observed provider responses and legal review override any unsupported implementation claim.

========== \prd.md ==========
# GHOOMI — Product Requirements Document (PRD)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Product | **Ghoomi** — smart travel planning, discovery, authorized booking and management |
| Tagline | **You Enjoy the Journey. Ghoomi Manages the Journey.** |
| Vision | **From Home to Home.** |
| Document revision | 1.0 — 2026-11-03 |
| Source basis | `reference/Ghoomi_Working_Platform_Master.pdf` (53 pages, 95 sections) |
| Current build scope | **Partition 1: Before First Deployment** |
| Status | Proposed implementation specification, **not** confirmation of implemented software |

> **Source fidelity.** The three partitions, named features, core workflows and the scope boundaries below come from Ghoomi's latest 53-page working-platform explanation. The requirement IDs, quantitative success measures, risk register, acceptance checks and launch-mode handling are **engineering/product recommendations** added to make the plan executable. Do not equate a proposed API with partner authorization.

## 1. Executive summary

Ghoomi aims to reduce the fragmented travel experience of researching flights, trains, buses, cabs, hotels, activities, restaurants and complete holiday schedules across many unrelated apps. The **initial release must resemble a credible travel discovery and comparison service** in its core user experience, but also provide genuine differentiators: useful AI-generated itineraries, transparent trip budgeting, easy plan editing and a centralized dashboard. Users can plan a complete journey even when some booking providers are not yet onboarded.

Ghoomi is **not** a guaranteed direct-booking agent for all services on day one. Where supported by authorized integrations, users may proceed to verified reservations or secure partner checkout. Other services must display their actual data/capability state without fictitious pricing, confirmations or checkout flows.

### 1.1 Product principles

1. **Unified journey over disconnected bookings.** A saved trip links departure, transport, stay, places, budget, planned activities and return.
2. **Familiar discovery, differentiated intelligence.** Clear service-specific search and comparison plus itinerary optimization and a trip hub.
3. **Customer controls decisions.** AI proposes, user edits; bookings, changes and costs require appropriate user approval.
4. **Trust is visible.** Source provenance, real/estimated price labels, last checked times, payment and booking status are never blurred.
5. **Progressive expansion.** Partition 1 launches a useful product; Partition 2 extends real operations; Partition 3 unlocks the full ecosystem.
6. **Modular choices.** Own Vehicle / Already Have Stay / no food planning remain valid options.
7. **Secure and accessible from release one.** Privacy, server-side authorization, responsive layout and error handling are foundational, not future premium extras.

## 2. Problems, personas and key jobs

| Persona | Primary job-to-be-done | Important constraints |
|---|---|---|
| Budget traveler / student | Find realistic affordable destination and trip options | Total cost, transport choice, flexibility |
| Solo traveler | Plan independently and see key safety information | Privacy, transparent results, accessible support |
| Friends / trip organizer | Compare options and organize an itinerary for several travelers | Correct traveler count, one saveable plan; full group collaboration later |
| Couple / family | Find suitable stays and activities for a chosen comfort level | Guest/room rules, timing, filters and budget |
| General traveler | Compare providers and return to a saved plan | Consistent sources and reliable last-saved state |
| Admin/support operator | Maintain trusted content, resolve reported issues and observe failures | Strict access control and auditability |
| Provider/partner (initially limited) | Expose permitted inventory or authorized booking flow | Agreement, API rights, provenance and current capability |

### 2.1 User pain points

- Searching multiple vendors repeats the same travel inputs; total trip spend remains unclear.
- Separate bookings are hard to connect with day-wise activity plans and return travel.
- Generic AI travel plans can be impractical, fabricated or not editable.
- Users struggle to compare transport/stay trade-offs and keep selected plans organized.
- Safety information and emergency contacts are often scattered.
- In later phases, itinerary changes, groups, family updates and refunds become complicated.

## 3. North-star experience and primary user journey

**Visitor** → explore destination or service → sign up / sign in for saved trip → provide origin, destination, dates, travelers, budget, preferences → choose Budget / Balanced / Comfort / Custom → generate or manually build itinerary → compare available transport/stay options → add restaurants and activities → edit and reorder the plan → view deterministic cost estimate → save or resume the trip → take **only supported** booking or partner-redirect actions → manage itinerary and confirmed/unverified references in the unified dashboard → travel and return. See `App Flow.md` for branch-by-branch navigation and error states.

### 3.1 Launch-mode classification

| Mode | What user can actually do | What must be shown |
|---|---|---|
| `PLANNING_ONLY` | Research and add a category to a plan based on verified editorial information or clearly labeled indicative estimates | `Planning / estimate only`, no fake Book button |
| `REDIRECT` | Follow authorized listing/affiliate link to provider site to complete booking | `Continue at provider`, clear provider responsibility |
| `LIVE_SEARCH_EXTERNAL_CHECKOUT` | Search live provider inventory, then complete eligible booking at provider | Provenance, quoted terms, last refreshed time |
| `DIRECT_BOOKING` | Complete supplier-authorized reservation through Ghoomi's supported backend | Real recheck, payment policy, confirmation/reference status |

**Do not assign a mode until it is proven by integration tests and legal/provider approval.** Modes may differ by category, market, partner and environment.

## 4. Product scope and priorities

### 4.1 Partition 1 — before first deployment (ACTIVE)

`P0`: essential to a usable first release. `P1`: intended initial-release supporting capabilities; feature can use a restricted truthful launch mode if external rights are not available.

| Requirement ID | Priority | Feature | V1 minimum behavior |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GHO-P1-AUTH` | P0 | Signup, login, recovery and session | Users own and secure their accounts; verification and recovery flows work |
| `GHO-P1-PROFILE` | P0 | Profile, travelers and preferences | Persist profile/traveler preferences with minimal necessary personal data |
| `GHO-P1-HOME` | P0 | Public website and global navigation | Distinct responsive travel discovery entry points and sign-in routes |
| `GHO-P1-DEST` | P0 | Destination discovery | Real/curated destination details, images with rights, place and guide content |
| `GHO-P1-TRIP` | P0 | Create, save, edit and resume trips | Origin, dates, travelers, preferences, budget and clear draft lifecycle |
| `GHO-P1-AI` | P0 | Smart planner and day-wise itinerary | Server-backed AI with structured validation, editable days and manual fallback |
| `GHO-P1-BUDGET` | P0 | Estimate and optimize budgets | Deterministic category totals, source labels, what-if scenarios and trade-offs |
| `GHO-P1-TRANSPORT` | P0 | Transport discovery | Flight, train, bus, cab/taxi, own-vehicle; genuine source/capability labels |
| `GHO-P1-STAY` | P0 | Accommodation discovery | Hotel/stay search, comparisons and available details/terms; existing stay path |
| `GHO-P1-EDIT` | P0 | Trip customization | Add/remove/reorder activities, alter choices/dates and recalc budgets |
| `GHO-P1-HUB` | P0 | My Trips and unified trip dashboard | Saved trip, selected services, itinerary, budget and booking statuses together |
| `GHO-P1-FOOD` | P1 | Restaurants and local food | Discover food options, filters and relevant location information; no fake ordering |
| `GHO-P1-ACT` | P1 | Attractions, activities and guides | Search/filter places and add them to itinerary with known time/cost metadata |
| `GHO-P1-BOOK` | P1 | Initial reservation/handoff | Authorized booking or provider redirect, status provenance and references |
| `GHO-P1-PAY` | P1 | Payment/cost summary | Transparent charges and verified statuses **only for enabled integrations** |
| `GHO-P1-SAFETY` | P1 | Basic safety | Emergency contacts, official emergency directory and travel safety tips |
| `GHO-P1-NOTIFY` | P1 | Basic notifications | Account, trip, booking and support events via configured approved channels |
| `GHO-P1-SUPPORT` | P1 | Customer support | Support form/ticket creation, status and admin review |
| `GHO-P1-ADMIN` | P1 | Admin foundation | Role-protected overview, content, tickets and relevant booking/provider records |
| `GHO-P1-PARTNER` | P1 | Initial provider adapters | Selected authorized integrations, capability matrix and health monitoring |
| `GHO-P1-QUALITY` | P0 | Release trust/reliability | AuthZ, responsive UI, error handling, testing, legal/product transparency, backups |

### 4.2 Partition 2 — after first deployment (NOT IN ACTIVE BUILD)

| ID | Module | Functional outcome / dependency |
|---|---|---|
| `GHO-P2-BOOK` | Expanded direct booking | More providers, stronger booking orchestration; requires real contracts and confirmed bookings |
| `GHO-P2-PAY` | Advanced payments/commissions | Verified gateway events, settlements, reconciliation, financial auditability |
| `GHO-P2-REFUND` | Modification/cancellation/refunds | Provider policies, user approval, confirmed refund statuses and support |

| `GHO-P2-DOC` | Booking documents | Private uploads, scanning, controlled access and retention |
