### 4.3 Partition 3 — long-term expansion (NOT IN ACTIVE BUILD)

| ID | Module | High-level goal |
|---|---|---|
| `GHO-P3-RECOVERY` | Intelligent trip recovery | Detect disruption → impact → verified alternatives → cost → approval → changes |
| `GHO-P3-HOME` | Back-to-Home | Coordinate an early return without auto-canceling or auto-paying |
| `GHO-P3-SOS` | Safety centre and SOS | Trusted-contact workflow, official information, incident handling; no false dispatch |
| `GHO-P3-ASSIST` | Advanced AI travel assistant | Contextual support grounded in live approved data and permissions |
| `GHO-P3-EVENTS` | Live events and destination intelligence | Current, source-verified listings and relevance ranking |
| `GHO-P3-WEATHER` | Weather-aware planning | Verified weather, appropriate alerts and activity alternatives |
| `GHO-P3-SPLIT` | Group expenses | Expense ledger, shares and settlement computation |
| `GHO-P3-OFFLINE` | Offline essentials | Previously synchronized travel data with freshness and limits |
| `GHO-P3-WALLET` | Secure document wallet | Protected sensitive documents, authorized access, deletion and audit |
| `GHO-P3-SOCIAL` | Public experiences/community | Explicit public opt-in, memories, reviews, itineraries and moderation |
| `GHO-P3-EXPLORE` | Ghoomi Explore | Curated/community trip discovery and itinerary inspiration |
| `GHO-P3-MARKET` | Partner marketplace | Verified partner listings, inventory/pricing and commissions |
| `GHO-P3-PREMIUM` | Membership | Optional validated premium entitlements and transparent billing |
| `GHO-P3-FINANCE` | Advanced financial operations | Commercial reconciliation and finance reporting subject to agreements |
| `GHO-P3-ANALYTICS` | Intelligence/analytics | Validated aggregate operational and product reporting |

## 5. Partition 1 detailed user stories and acceptance criteria

### 5.1 Identity and users — `GHO-P1-AUTH`, `GHO-P1-PROFILE`

**Stories.** As a visitor, I can create an account and securely return to my plans. As an authenticated traveler, I can maintain preferences and add travelers without exposing those profiles to other accounts.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Signup accepts only validated fields; duplicate account handling does not disclose private account state unnecessarily.
- [ ] Login/logout, password reset and email verification function end-to-end with expiry/replay protection appropriate to implementation.
- [ ] Server rejects unauthenticated access to private trip/traveler resources; cross-account UUID requests return no data.
- [ ] User can edit profile, default budget range, food/travel interests and save limited traveler records; booking-specific data is requested only when required.
- [ ] Account recovery, input error, rate limiting and session expiry have clear user-facing states.

### 5.2 Destination, discovery and home — `GHO-P1-HOME`, `GHO-P1-DEST`

**Stories.** As a visitor, I can find destinations, open guides and launch trip planning from useful, filterable content.

- [ ] Navigation contains meaningful entrances to flights, trains, buses, cabs, hotels, holiday planning, food and activities.
- [ ] Destination guide includes overview, attractions, relevant map/location context, useful tips and verified/curated source labels.
- [ ] Search supports no-results, network failures, location ambiguity and date corrections without losing submitted criteria.
- [ ] Images/content have acceptable licensing; unsupported current events/hours are labeled unknown rather than fabricated.

### 5.3 Trip wizard and saved trips — `GHO-P1-TRIP`

**Story.** As a traveler, I can make a trip once and return to it rather than re-entering everything.

- [ ] Inputs: source, destination, start/end, traveler count/types, budget/currency, travel type, pace, interests, transport/stay preferences, optional departure/return times.
- [ ] Start date <= end date; impossible/invalid traveler counts and incompatible options receive actionable validation.
- [ ] Every meaningful change can be saved; reloading or signing in again restores the correct user's data.
- [ ] Own Vehicle and Already Have Stay skip irrelevant external booking steps but still contribute relevant planned costs.
- [ ] State supports `DRAFT`, `PLANNED`, `ACTIVE`, `COMPLETED`, `CANCELLED` with validated transitions and audit metadata.

### 5.4 AI itinerary — `GHO-P1-AI`

**Story.** As a trip planner, I receive a logical, adjustable itinerary that respects duration, preferences and known constraints.

- [ ] Backend sends only authorized minimum trip context to configured AI provider; no API keys/client secrets exposed.
- [ ] Output is structured by day with place/title, proposed start/end, estimated travel time/cost, type and uncertainty/source fields.
- [ ] Validate chronology, date coverage, impossible durations and obviously contradictory activities; if invalid, repair safely or report a recoverable error.
- [ ] Users can accept selected days, reject recommendations, reorder items and regenerate with explicit indication of what may change.
- [ ] AI service error/timeout yields manual template or existing saved itinerary, **not** a blank or fabricated successful response.
- [ ] Never claim an AI suggestion is proof of availability, opening hours, price, reservation or weather.

### 5.5 Budget engine — `GHO-P1-BUDGET`

**Story.** As a budget-conscious traveler, I see how transport, stay, food and activities affect the complete cost.

- [ ] Categories include outbound/return transport, stay, meals, local travel, attractions, taxes/fees if verified, emergency reserve and optional miscellaneous.
- [ ] Compute `estimated_total = sum(estimated_line_items)` with integer paise and clear currency; `remaining = user_budget - estimated_total`.
- [ ] Show amounts as `estimated`, `provider-quoted`, `paid` (where verified) or `user-entered`; never merge states silently.
- [ ] Budget/Balanced/Comfort/Custom plans allow category-level trade-off; explain savings assumptions and impacts.
- [ ] Changes to travelers, dates, offer selection and itinerary recalculate estimates deterministically and preserve a revision trail where practicable.

### 5.6 Transport discovery — `GHO-P1-TRANSPORT`

**Story.** As a user, I can compare relevant transport modes without confusing estimated results with confirmed inventory.

- [ ] Flight inputs: departure/arrival, dates, passengers; results show carrier, times, duration, terms and verified/estimated price provenance.
- [ ] Train inputs: origin/destination stations, date, class; schedules/fares/availability appear only from authorized data. Booking may require authorized rail partnership.
- [ ] Bus inputs: source/destination/date, operator, bus type, boarding/drop points and eligible availability.
- [ ] Cab inputs: pickup/drop, time, type and local/airport/outstation options; show estimated vs partner-confirmed quote accurately.
- [ ] Own Vehicle option creates route/cost planning rather than fake booking.
- [ ] Filters/sorting operate on actual returned attributes and preserve the search criteria; unsupported verticals show actionable honest fallbacks.

### 5.7 Stays and services — `GHO-P1-STAY`, `GHO-P1-FOOD`, `GHO-P1-ACT`

- [ ] Search accommodation by destination, stay dates, guests, rooms and budget; result cards show reliable location, facilities, prices and cancellation conditions where available.
- [ ] Detail and room selection distinguish property editorial descriptions from live room inventory; user can use own/family accommodation.
- [ ] Restaurants: cuisine, location, price band and available provider-derived details; discovery only unless ordering is explicitly integrated.
- [ ] Activities: attractions, experience type, duration and known fees; can add/remove activities from trip without needing a ticketing integration.
- [ ] Plans handle missing reviews, missing open-now data, provider outages and no matching offers gracefully.

### 5.8 Trip customization and hub — `GHO-P1-EDIT`, `GHO-P1-HUB`

- [ ] User can add/remove/rearrange itinerary items and selected services; revisions save and are scoped to the owner.
- [ ] Dashboard aggregates title, dates, travelers, all selected services, cost estimate, saved items, status/confirmed bookings and next planned activity.
- [ ] Clear separation among `planned itinerary`, `external unverified reference`, `provider-confirmed booking` and `paid` amounts.
- [ ] No stale UI after edit, page refresh or resuming from My Trips; long or complex trips remain mobile usable.

### 5.9 Initial booking, payment and operations — `GHO-P1-BOOK`, `GHO-P1-PAY`, `GHO-P1-NOTIFY`, `GHO-P1-SUPPORT`, `GHO-P1-ADMIN`, `GHO-P1-PARTNER`

- [ ] Each enabled provider has documented capability/authorization; quote or availability revalidated before eligible checkout.
- [ ] Redirect mode identifies provider and responsibility. Direct mode records distinct request/processing/confirmed/failed states and supplier reference.
- [ ] Monetary operations are impossible without verified gateway setup, server-side callbacks/webhook verification, idempotency and auditability.
- [ ] User sees provider price, applicable fees, taxes and final amount; changed price requires acceptance.
- [ ] Support tickets save and show status; administrators can process within authorized access; notifications reflect actual events and delivery results.
- [ ] Admin panel protects privileged routes, supports basic content/provider/ticket oversight, and logs important actions.
- [ ] If no partner credentials exist, provide an honest planning or supported redirect experience and explicitly mark provider integration `Blocked`; never simulate production success.

### 5.10 Basic safety and platform quality — `GHO-P1-SAFETY`, `GHO-P1-QUALITY`

- [ ] User can store/revise emergency contacts and view relevant official emergency guidance with source/location context.
- [ ] V1 does **not** promise dispatch, guaranteed live tracking or family monitoring; those are later partitions.
- [ ] UI is keyboard navigable, responsive, usable on mobile, resilient to retries/network failure and properly labeled.
- [ ] Automated auth/account-isolation, money calculation, booking-truth, API schema and E2E user-flow tests pass in staging.
- [ ] Production operations have backups/restore rehearsal, log redaction, alert routes, rollback plan and privacy/legal pre-launch review.

## 6. Functional and nonfunctional boundaries

### In scope for first deployment

A complete **plan → compare → customize → estimate → save → supported checkout or provider handoff → manage trip** journey. Booking and payment operate only to the depth actually authorized; a reliable planning service can launch with some non-bookable categories clearly labeled.

### Explicitly out of scope for first deployment

- Broad direct supplier inventory ownership and third-party checkout circumvention.
- Automated multi-provider refund/cancellation orchestration or guaranteed refund times.
- Live GPS/family dashboard, continuous background location or emergency dispatch.
- Intelligent disruption rebooking / Back-to-Home auto execution.
- Shared group wallet, public social feed, partner ERP/marketplace and premium subscription billing.

### Proposed initial quality targets (validate/replace with real data)

| Dimension | Proposed beta launch gate |
|---|---|
| Account isolation | 0 reproducible cross-account resource access vulnerabilities |
| Saved trip integrity | >=99% successful persistence in controlled beta under nominal test conditions |
| AI fallback | 100% of tested AI-unavailable cases allow manual editing/saving |
| Booking representation | 0 fabricated `CONFIRMED` statuses in tests; every confirmation has provider evidence |
| Responsive UX | Main user flows checked at ~360px, 768px, 1280px widths |
| Accessibility | Automated checks and manual keyboard/screen-reader smoke tests on high-traffic flows |
| Operations | Backup restore, rollback and alert escalation exercises completed |
| Content | Each public price/inventory claim shows source and freshness or clear uncertainty |

These are targets, **not performance claims**.

## 7. Example acceptance scenario

**Journey:** four friends plan a five-day Mumbai-to-Goa trip with an INR 30,000 budget.

1. User registers and creates trip with date range, four travelers and category preferences.
2. User generates a day-wise itinerary and chooses more comfortable accommodation but cheaper transport.
3. Ghoomi explains revised estimated spend, remaining budget, assumptions and which items have genuine supplier quotes.
4. User filters hotels and transport, selects supported options or own transport/stay, and saves the itinerary.
5. Dashboard retains accurate activities and estimate after logout/login.
6. If booking integration is authorized, user reviews rechecked quote, fees and terms, follows correct checkout and receives only verified reservation status. If not, the app shows `Planning only` or clearly describes a provider handoff.
7. User can access relevant emergency information and support without fictitious live monitoring.

This scenario becomes a core automated E2E acceptance test and fixture, clearly labeled as **test data**.

## 8. Business model — planned, not contracted

The intended initial business model is **partner commissions** on eligible completed bookings, with possible disclosed platform/convenience fees where permitted. No commission rate, service charge, GST calculation method or direct payment flow is finalized. Partner agreements and legal/payment review must precede any commercial claims. Additional premium, featured listing and marketplace revenue belong to later validation stages.

## 9. Metrics and product learning

Instrument with privacy-aware analytics: destination searches, search-result usefulness, trip wizard completion, itinerary generation/fallback, saves, edits, budget category adjustments, partner handoffs, **verified** booking confirmations (separate from clicks), failures by provider, support cases and retention. Segment only at appropriate aggregate/privacy-safe levels. Review beta findings before prioritizing Partition 2.

## 10. Risks and mitigations

| Risk | Mitigation / launch decision |
|---|---|
| Provider APIs unavailable, expensive or restricted | Capability matrix; truthful planning/approved redirect mode; begin with 1–2 reliable verticals |
| Railway and hotel integrations require special approval | Secure explicit rights/terms; no unauthorized ticket issuance or room guarantee |
| AI generates impractical itinerary | Structured output, server validation, source attribution, manual edits/fallback |
| Estimates appear as guarantees | Provider source labels, age/terms display, recheck before checkout |
| Payments and bookings mismatch | Independent states, verified events, idempotency, reconciliation and support path |
| Too many initial pages | Group 55 inventory entries into reusable route families and end-to-end slices |
| Safety expectations exceed browser/provider abilities | Basic emergency information first; consent-based future tracking; honest limitations |
| Sensitive traveler data leaked | Data minimization, authorization tests, encryption and audited access |
| Thin support capacity | Pilot geography, controlled rollout, visible support/responsibility boundaries |

## 11. Definition of Partition 1 release-ready

A release is not ready merely because pages visually exist. All **P0** items must have tested user flows, persistence and protection; P1 items must either work as promised or be transparently restricted to the authorized capability. Validate the 17 first-release criteria in the source (section 43), plus rules in `rules.md` and release gates in `Implementation plan.md`. Every gate outcome must have evidence in `tracker.md`.

## 12. Traceability and change policy

- UI routes and detailed behavior: `App Flow.md` and `UI-UX design Brief.md`.
- API/provider contracts and stack: `trd.md`.
- Database entities and migrations: `Backend Schema.md`.
- Execution sequence: `Implementation plan.md`.
- Work/evidence and readiness: `tracker.md`.
- Decision history/unknowns: `memory.md`.
- Binding agent policies: `rules.md` + `AGENTS.md`.

**Scope change:** amend this PRD feature row/ID, related flow/contract/schema and tracker; log the approved decision in `memory.md`. Avoid independent, drifting copies of the feature plan.

# GHOOMI — Agent-ready product specification pack

**Version:** 1.1 — MySQL edition | **Prepared:** 2026-11-03 | **Current build target:** Partition 1 — before first deployment.

Inspect my existing codebase first. Tell me what already exists, any gaps/conflicts, the first approved P0 slice and its test plan; then implement the smallest complete slice. Update the tracker with measured evidence only. Do not fabricate integrations, live prices, bookings or payments.

========== \rules.md ==========
# GHOOMI — Enforceable development rules

**Applies:** every agent change, feature, API, query and deployment. **Classification:** product/engineering guardrails; source-aligned safety principles plus implementation recommendations. If a request conflicts with a higher-priority safety/legal obligation, stop the conflicting action and explain the blocker.

## A. Scope and truthfulness

| Rule | Non-negotiable behavior |
|---|---|
| R-01 | Implement **Partition 1** first. Never build a Partition 2/3 feature unless explicitly requested and the user understands dependencies. |
| R-02 | A visible button must perform its described action or be clearly unavailable with a reason. No dead links or pretend checkout. |
| R-03 | No invented fares, available seats/rooms, weather alerts, opening hours, booking IDs, payment results or provider endorsements. |
| R-04 | Label each displayed offer: `verified live`, `provider-listed`, `indicative estimate`, or `unavailable`, together with source and last-checked time where known. Never infer “live” from cached data. |
| R-05 | Show capability mode: `PLANNING_ONLY`, `REDIRECT`, `LIVE_SEARCH_EXTERNAL_CHECKOUT`, `DIRECT_BOOKING`; disable unsupported actions per provider. |
| R-06 | AI output is advisory. Validate schema, dates, trip-length, activity scheduling and location assumptions server-side. Provide manual/deterministic fallback when AI is down. |
| R-07 | User approval is required before charges, paid upgrades, cancellations, policy-impacting changes or any automated rebooking. |
| R-08 | Do not state a reservation is `CONFIRMED` without authoritative provider evidence; payment `SUCCEEDED` requires verified gateway/provider evidence, not client navigation. |
| R-09 | Costs show original provider amount, verified/estimated taxes and fees, discounts where genuine, total currency and per-person-vs-total basis. |
| R-10 | Unknown data remains unknown. An incomplete vendor response is not permission to guess missing information. |

## B. Security and privacy

- **R-11: Auth and authorization.** Passwords hashed with a modern adaptive password hash; secure session lifecycle; every privileged resource endpoint enforces object-level authorization *server-side*. Access by guessing UUID is prohibited.
- **R-12: Minimize PII.** Only collect booking details as required by a real service; identity documents and payment card data must not be stored by default. Use processor tokenization where available.
- **R-13: Consent.** Any future location sharing requires explicit, revocable, scoped consent, recipient identity, purpose, expiry and timestamps; location sharing off by default.
- **R-14: Safety.** V1 has emergency contacts and trusted official information; never display “Help dispatched” without a verified responder relationship. No guaranteed real-time tracking in a normal browser.
- **R-15: Secrets.** Private keys, provider tokens, webhook signing secrets and AI keys stay on backend, never in public web bundles or Markdown. `.env.example` can contain variable names only.
- **R-16: App security.** Validate request payloads; prevent SQL injection, XSS and CSRF as applicable; rate-limit auth, AI and financial operations; restrict CORS; audit high-risk actions; scrub PII/secrets from logs.
- **R-17: Data lifecycle.** Create documented deletion, retention, backup and restoration procedures; verify appropriate India privacy/consumer/travel/payment legal duties before commercial operation.
- **R-18: Content provenance.** Do not scrape or reuse third-party logos, photos, pricing or inventory contrary to licensing/terms; disclose sponsored offers and affiliate relationships.

## C. Provider and payment reliability

- **R-19:** Each provider uses an adapter with a capability matrix, timeout, bounded retry/backoff, rate handling, normalization, observable errors and kill switch.
- **R-20:** Before eligible checkout, request reprice/reavailability and require acceptance if terms or amounts change. Do not turn stale estimates into instant checkout.
- **R-21:** Payment and booking state machines are independent. Webhooks require signature/authenticity verification where available, replay detection and idempotent processing.
- **R-22:** Retry financial operations only when idempotent and safe. Store idempotency keys and reconcile unknown timeouts; never double-charge or double-book.
- **R-23:** Redirect checkout must disclose that the provider processes the reservation, and Ghoomi cannot claim success without proof or a verified integration path.
- **R-24:** On supplier outage, surface clear status and permit unrelated trip planning, manual alternatives and saved itineraries.

## D. Engineering delivery

- **R-25:** Inspect actual code and constraints first; do not rebuild, wipe DB, rewrite dependencies or change repo config destructively without an approved reason.
- **R-26:** Implement end-to-end feature slices with typed contracts, input validation, persistence, authorization, UI states, tests and telemetry; avoid wide shallow mockups.
- **R-27:** **MySQL is the user-selected database.** Use MySQL 8.4 LTS/InnoDB compatible DDL and reversible/versioned Flyway migrations; add explicit indexes/constraints and keep seed data tagged non-production. Do not add PostgreSQL-only syntax, silently switch databases or introduce schema drift.
- **R-28:** A feature is `Done` only with evidence in `tracker.md`: exact test commands, actual results, required acceptance checks and no open critical defect.
- **R-29:** Accessibility is a release requirement: visible focus, semantic controls, keyboard usage, form error announcements, reduced motion, sufficient contrast.
- **R-30:** Maintain docs and traceability: mention `GHO-...` product requirements, API contracts and `IMP-...` plan task IDs in work summaries/PRs.
- **R-31:** Protect production data. Use separate development, staging, production; never run destructive migrations or tests against production without explicit authorization.
- **R-32:** Secure CI and automated tests; run backend validation, UI build/types/lint, DB migration checks, auth negative tests, financial failure cases (if applicable), and at least one complete trip path.
- **R-33:** Roll out behind feature flags for provider-sensitive modules; log incidents and rollback procedures before public launch.
- **R-34:** Use feature IDs and stable interfaces; prefer a modular monolith until measured needs justify microservices.

## E. Agent operating protocol

1. Read `AGENTS.md` → `rules.md` → `memory.md` → `tracker.md` before major work. Locate related acceptance criteria in `prd.md`.
2. State goal, scope, dependencies, potential external approvals and exact tests.
3. Inspect repository and do not assume technology decisions are already implemented.
4. Implement a small vertical slice using the contracts in `trd.md` and `Backend Schema.md`.
5. Run tests; report failures, unrun checks and provider limitations truthfully.
6. Update `tracker.md` item status and evidence; update `memory.md` only for decisions or new durable truths. Commit only when requested/authorized.

## F. Conflict resolution / change control

**Source hierarchy:** user-approved changed requirements → 53-page master partition policy → `prd.md`/`rules.md` → technical designs → tracker. Safety/financial truthfulness are never weakened by mere UI requests. When two docs contradict, record a `DEC-...` in `memory.md`, do not silently choose one for a security-sensitive or scope-changing decision, and keep unfinished work blocked.


========== \tracker.md ==========
# GHOOMI — Live Project Tracker

**Tracking baseline:** 2026-11-03 • **Active roadmap:** Partition 1 — before first deployment • **Product software status:** **NOT AUDITED / NOT VERIFIED.** This file is a template and initial backlog, **not evidence of completed development**.

> Coding agents must update only after inspecting the repository and actually running the listed tests. Documentation has been drafted, but no claim is made that the app exists, that APIs work, that partners are authorized or that Ghoomi is deployed. All implementation items start `Not started`. Use task IDs shared with `Implementation plan.md`.

## 1. Status legend

| Status | Meaning | Evidence requirement |
|---|---|---|
| `Not started` | No verified implementation or no audit yet | None; default |
| `In progress` | Actual active work, feature still incomplete | Branch/files and scoped task |
| `Blocked` | Cannot safely proceed; dependency missing | Explicit blocker, owner/next action |
| `Implemented` | Code exists but not fully validated | Relevant files/commit and known limits |
| `Tested` | Acceptance checks passed in named environment | Commands, outputs, test runs, evidence |
| `Deployed` | Verified running in staging/production | URL/environment, deployment version and smoke proof |

A work item may be `Implemented` without being `Tested` or `Deployed`. **Never** skip proof between status stages. Add no arbitrary completion percentages; calculate only from verified statuses.

| Milestone | Status | Release significance | Exit criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| M0 — audit & decisions | Tested | P0 prerequisite | Repo inspected, stack/provider modes confirmed |

| M2 — trip and editorial discovery | Not started | P0 | Persisted trip + useful destination catalog |

| M5 — safety/support/admin | Not started | P1 | Useful basic help and staff operation |
| M6 — release hardening and deployment | Not started | Release gate | Testing, partner compliance, backups, staged rollout |
| Partition 2 expansion | Future / not authorized | Post-first-deployment | Requires user approval and operational readiness |
| Partition 3 ecosystem | Future / not authorized | Long-term | Requires business validation and dependencies |

- [ ] REL-13: Admin can perform authorized essential operations.
- [ ] REL-14: API/provider/AI failures handled safely.

**Additional production guardrails:** no critical auth/financial defect, provider source rights, data retention policy, backup restore, monitoring/alert escalation, rollback and clear live-vs-estimated labels.

## 10. Daily session log / agent handoff

## 11. Change control / handoff discipline

- If a new feature changes release partition, record approval in `memory.md` and update `prd.md`, `App Flow.md`, `UI-UX design Brief.md`, `trd.md`, `Backend Schema.md` and this tracker as applicable.
- If an API/provider is unavailable, mark the relevant task `Blocked` and implement only an honest fallback. Do not claim it is ready merely because its mock form appears.
- Documentation completion and software completion are **different metrics**; do not conflate them.
- At end of every coding session, summarize exact finished work, tests, blockers and next step without promising unattended background action.

- **Evidence:** `memory.md` ADR-001, `trd.md`, `Backend Schema.md`, `database/mysql/V1__ghoomi_partition1_reference.sql`.


========== \trd.md ==========
# GHOOMI — Technical Requirements Document (TRD)

**Revision:** 1.0 • **Build target:** Partition 1 (first release) • **Source:** 53-page Ghoomi working-platform master, especially sections 10, 41, 59, 80, 82–94. **Architecture and tooling details here are proposed technical decisions**, not claims of existing implementation or supplier approval.

## 1. Engineering objective and constraints

Implement a trustworthy, production-oriented travel discovery and planning service that saves user-specific trips, generates editable AI itineraries, computes budgets deterministically, and connects to **authorized** provider services through a common contract. The first version must remain useful even when some travel providers do not offer direct-booking access.

**Non-negotiable technical qualities:** secure identity; per-resource authorization; clearly labeled estimate vs verified data; no invented inventory/booking/payment data; accurate persistence; graceful vendor/AI failure; responsive accessible UI; observable operations and restore/rollback plan.

## 2. Proposed stack and architecture decision

### 2.1 Preferred baseline *only for a new/empty repo* (`ADR-001 — Proposed`)

| Layer | Proposed choice | Why / note |
|---|---|---|
| Frontend | Next.js App Router + React + TypeScript | SEO-friendly travel catalog and application UI; keep client/server boundaries clear |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS + accessible primitives | Reusable design system defined in `UI-UX design Brief.md` |
| Backend | Java Spring Boot modular monolith, Spring Web, Validation and Security | Source document suggests Java Spring Boot; suitable domain modules and provider adapters |
| Database | MySQL 8.4 LTS (InnoDB) with Flyway migrations | Approved database; relational trip/booking/financial consistency, JSON only when justified |
| ORM | Spring Data JPA for core transactional entities | Avoid overcomplicated query layers; use explicit SQL for complex reporting |
| Authentication | Spring Security with HTTP-only secure session cookies (recommended), or approved token approach | Never put long-lived secrets in JS-accessible storage; decide cross-domain strategy |
| AI | Backend proxy to approved LLM with structured response schema | Configurable provider; constraints, output validation, budget calculation external to AI |
| Provider APIs | Adapter per vertical/provider behind capability registry | Approved rights, throttling, normalization, source/freshness metadata |
| Background tasks | DB-backed scheduled jobs initially; queue later if needed | Reconciliation, notifications, expiration, cache refresh |
| Object storage | Private encrypted bucket when approved documents are required | V1 may not need sensitive document storage |
| Observability | Structured logs, metrics, trace IDs, centralized error tracking | Booking/AI/provider failures and privacy-safe operation |
| CI/CD | Git/GitHub, backend build/tests, frontend build/lint/types, staging + controlled prod | No direct untested changes to production |

**Important:** source lists React/Next.js, Spring Boot and MySQL/PostgreSQL as **choices**; the user has now specifically selected **MySQL**. If a substantial working React/Vite or Node project exists, inspect it and obtain approval before migration. This pack recommends an architecture for consistency, **not mandatory destructive rewriting**.

### 2.2 Deployment topology

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  U[Web client] --> CDN[Web hosting / CDN]
  CDN --> FE[Next.js frontend]
  FE -->|HTTPS authorized calls| API[Spring Boot REST API]
  API --> AUTH[Identity and access module]
  API --> TRIP[Trip + itinerary + budget]
  API --> SEARCH[Discovery + provider adapter registry]
  API --> BOOK[Booking + payment status]
  API --> SUPPORT[Admin + support + notifications]
  API --> AI[AI plan orchestration]
  API --> MYSQL[(MySQL 8.4)]
  SEARCH --> EX[Authorized supplier / maps / catalog APIs]
  BOOK --> PAY[Authorized provider/payment gateway]
  AI --> LLM[Approved AI provider]
  API --> METRIC[Logs / metrics / alerts]
```

All external supplier and AI secrets are server-only. The UI must not call private supplier APIs directly unless an explicitly approved public client SDK/API model supports it.

### 2.3 Suggested domain module boundaries

`identity`, `profiles`, `destinations`, `trips`, `itineraries`, `budget`, `search`, `providers`, `booking`, `payment-status`, `ai-planning`, `notifications`, `safety-directory`, `support`, `admin`, `audit`.

Each module owns its DTOs/business logic and reaches other modules through interfaces, not direct coupling to provider SDKs or UI state. Monolith until measured operational need justifies service separation.

## 3. Repositories, environments and configuration

Suggested layout if empty:

```text
web/                 # Next.js frontend
api/                 # Spring Boot modules, controllers, services, tests
  src/main/java/...  # domain-focused packages
  src/main/resources/db/migration/  # Flyway SQL
infrastructure/      # deployment templates, observability, scripts
reference/           # source master PDF
AGENTS.md + *.md     # project specs, tracking and rules
```

**Environment separation:** `local`, `test`, `staging`, `production`, with separate DBs/API keys/service accounts. Variables such as `DATABASE_URL`, `SESSION_SECRET`, `AI_PROVIDER_API_KEY`, `MAPS_API_KEY`, `PAYMENT_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `SUPPLIER_<NAME>_TOKEN` appear only in `.env.example` by name, never with real values. Never log production secrets; prefer secret manager. Turn all provider-sensitive features off by default until validated.

## 4. Technical functional requirements by module

### `API-AUTH` — identity / security (`GHO-P1-AUTH`, `PROFILE`)

Signup/login/logout/password reset/verification; encrypted transport, modern password hashing, rate limiting and secure cookie/session rotation. Authenticated role from server records, not untrusted client claims. Scope trips and related data to owner; admin support access is explicit and audited. Saved traveler records store only needed details until booking requires more.

### `API-TRIP` — planning data (`TRIP`, `EDIT`, `HUB`)

Trip CRUD with version number, trip membership/owner (V1 owner-first), itinerary days/items, selected offers or own arrangements and lifecycle state. Support `draft`, `planned`, `active`, `completed`, `cancelled` with server validation. All mutating endpoints reject concurrent stale revisions with `409 VERSION_CONFLICT` or documented merge policy. No cancellation side effect on paid supplier booking.

### `API-DEST` — destination editorial catalog (`DEST`, `FOOD`, `ACT`)

Destination records and place information including content license/source. Curated data is not “live”; current schedules, restaurant opening hours, maps and prices require appropriate provider/public-data attribution. Pagination, full-text search, filters and cache-control appropriate to source.

### `API-SEARCH` — provider adapter gateway (`TRANSPORT`, `STAY`, `PARTNER`)

Normalize supplier responses while preserving source fields/terms. **Adapter modes:** `PLANNING_ONLY`, `REDIRECT`, `LIVE_SEARCH_EXTERNAL_CHECKOUT`, `DIRECT_BOOKING`. Provider registry stores business authorization/health flags. Filter/sort based on returned values; don't produce fake listings when no supplier is configured. Rate limit, timeout and backoff. Cache permitted read data with TTL and last-checked timestamp, never claim stale responses are live.

### `API-AI` — itinerary suggestions (`AI`, `BUDGET`)

Validate input trip scope, collect destination/POI context only from permitted sources, submit schema-guided prompt via server-only AI key, parse structured output, validate day boundaries and constraints, store run/result provenance and explain unknown assumptions. Regeneration requires explicit target (whole plan or day). If AI fails, return `AI_UNAVAILABLE` plus deterministic/manual starter; do not fill with fabricated supplier facts.

**AI must not be the calculator of record.** `budget` module calculates categories from normalized integer money line items, with estimate provenance and versioning.

### `API-BOOK` — provider booking intent, payment and support (`BOOK`, `PAY`)

A booking intent starts only when the supplier capability permits. Recheck selection (price/availability, terms); require fresh acceptance on changed quotes. For provider redirect: create optional traceable handoff, but status stays `REDIRECTED_EXTERNAL`/unverified without a supported callback. Direct bookings use idempotency keys, stored supplier request/response references, separate booking and payment state machines, verified webhook authenticity, and reconciliation. Do not implement payment collection unless gateway/partner/legal/operational gates pass.

### `API-OPS` — notification, safety, support and admin

Notification dispatch records actual channel statuses; basic local emergency information and emergency contacts only, no V1 SOS automation. Support tickets with status/owner/assignment and privacy controls. Admin APIs use explicit admin policy and audit logs; no global user-data access by ordinary users.

## 5. API contract conventions

**Base path:** `/api/v1`. **Format:** JSON (UTF-8), `snake_case` DB but `camelCase` JSON recommended. **Time:** UTC ISO 8601 in API; local timezone stored with itinerary as IANA timezone when relevant. **Money:** `{ "amountMinor": 189900, "currency": "INR" }`; minor INR units are paise. Use int64, no binary floats. **IDs:** UUID, opaque. **Pagination:** `cursor`/`limit` for collections; filtering encoded safely. **Writes:** validate at boundary; return structured error.

Standard success envelope may be direct JSON; choose once. Standard error:

```json
{
  "error": {
    "code": "PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE",
    "message": "Search is temporarily unavailable for this provider.",
    "requestId": "req_example",
    "retryable": true,
    "details": []
  }
}
```

**HTTP semantics:** `400` validation, `401` unauthenticated, `403` forbidden, `404` not found (resource-masking policy consistent), `409` version/quote conflict, `422` invalid business constraints, `429` rate limited, `502/503` supplier/AI unavailable. No success on failed side effect. Consumer-facing errors cannot reveal internal secret/provider details.

### 5.1 Endpoint inventory (proposed)

| API group | Method and route | Inputs → outputs | Auth |
|---|---|---|---|
| AUTH | `POST /auth/signup`, `/auth/login`, `/auth/logout`, `/auth/verify`, `/auth/password-reset` | Validated identity → session/result | As appropriate |
| PROFILE | `GET/PATCH /me`, `GET/POST /me/travellers`, `PATCH/DELETE /me/travellers/{id}` | Profile/travelers | User |
| DEST | `GET /destinations`, `/destinations/{slug}`, `GET /places` | Search/filter → sourced catalog | Public |
| TRIP | `GET/POST /trips`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /trips/{id}` | Saved trip with version/status | Owner |
| TRIP | `GET/PUT /trips/{id}/preferences` | Preferences and plan mode | Owner |
| PLAN | `POST /trips/{id}/plans/generate`, `GET /trips/{id}/plans` | AI request → `aiRunId` + structured plan/status | Owner |
| PLAN | `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /trips/{id}/itinerary/...` | Itinerary day/item CRUD | Owner |
| BUDGET | `GET/PUT /trips/{id}/budget`, `POST /trips/{id}/budget/recalculate` | Deterministic items and totals | Owner |
| SEARCH | `GET /providers/capabilities`, `POST /search/{vertical}` | Criteria → normalized offers/status | Public w/rate limit |
| SEARCH | `GET /offers/{offerId}` | Detail + validity, only if backed by allowed source | Public |
| BOOK | `POST /booking-intents`, `POST /booking-intents/{id}/reprice` | Eligible selection → quote + status | Owner, capability gate |
| BOOK | `POST /booking-intents/{id}/confirm`, `GET /bookings/{id}` | Idempotent book + verified state | Owner, direct mode only |
| BOOK | `POST /booking-intents/{id}/redirect` | Approved external handoff | User if persisted |
| PAY | `POST /payments/initiate`, `GET /payments/{id}` | Only when gateway enabled | Owner, feature flag |
| HOOK | `POST /webhooks/{provider}` | Signature-verified provider event | Server-to-server only |
| SAFETY | `GET /safety/resources`, `GET/POST /me/emergency-contacts` | Local info/trusted contact | Public/user as applicable |
| SUPPORT | `POST/GET /support/tickets`, `GET /support/tickets/{id}` | Support tickets | Owner/assigned staff |
| ADMIN | `GET /admin/overview`, `GET /admin/providers`, `/admin/tickets` | Authorized aggregates / workflows | Admin-only |

Endpoints are a **contract proposal**, not proof of an existing API. Document exact OpenAPI DTOs before implementing a production integration.

### 5.2 Sample normalized offer (`API-SEARCH`)

```json
{
  "id": "offer-ref-opaque",
  "vertical": "FLIGHT",
  "providerId": "provider-ref-opaque",
  "providerName": "Configured supplier",
  "capability": "LIVE_SEARCH_EXTERNAL_CHECKOUT",
  "dataStatus": "PROVIDER_LISTED",
  "originCode": "BOM",
  "destinationCode": "GOI",
  "departAt": "2026-12-12T06:30:00+05:30",
  "arriveAt": "2026-12-12T07:50:00+05:30",
  "price": { "amountMinor": 540000, "currency": "INR", "basis": "PER_PERSON" },
  "feeBreakdownAvailable": false,
  "checkedAt": "2026-11-03T08:30:00Z",
  "validUntil": null,
  "termsUrl": null,
  "nextAction": "CONTINUE_AT_PROVIDER"
}
```

**The sample is illustrative only, not a real fare or available itinerary.** No card may show this as live data without configured verified supplier response. Null terms/fees must show `Not provided` and may block direct checkout.

### 5.3 Sample trip and AI plan DTOs

```json
{
  "tripId": "uuid",
  "version": 2,
  "title": "Goa holiday",
  "origin": { "placeId": "verified-place-id", "label": "Mumbai" },
  "destination": { "placeId": "verified-place-id", "label": "Goa" },
  "startDate": "2026-12-12",
  "endDate": "2026-12-16",
  "timezone": "Asia/Kolkata",
  "travelerCount": 4,
  "planMode": "BALANCED",
  "budget": { "amountMinor": 3000000, "currency": "INR" },
  "status": "DRAFT"
}
```

AI response schema (conceptual): `runId`, `status`, `sourceType=AI_SUGGESTION`, `days[{date,items[{title,category,plannedStart,plannedEnd,estimatedCost,placeRef?,verificationStatus,notes}]}]`, `warnings[]`. Do not allow AI to emit a `CONFIRMED_BOOKING` field.

## 6. Booking/payment state transitions and reconciliation

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
  [*] --> DRAFT_INTENT
  DRAFT_INTENT --> REDIRECTED_EXTERNAL: approved redirect
  DRAFT_INTENT --> PENDING: approved direct booking
  PENDING --> PROCESSING
  PROCESSING --> CONFIRMED: verified provider confirmation
  PROCESSING --> FAILED: definitive failure
  PROCESSING --> UNKNOWN: timeout / uncertain response
  UNKNOWN --> CONFIRMED: reconciliation evidence
  UNKNOWN --> FAILED: verified failure
  REDIRECTED_EXTERNAL --> [*]
  CONFIRMED --> [*]
```

**Payment state:** `NOT_STARTED/INITIATED/PENDING/SUCCEEDED/FAILED/UNKNOWN`. Booking cannot become confirmed merely because payment succeeded; payment cannot become succeeded merely because provider booking exists. If a redirect has no approved callback or user-verifiable import, keep the record unverified.

Provider webhooks: authenticate/signature validate → dedupe provider event ID → store receipt → enqueue/execute idempotent processor → lock and transition allowed records → audit → notify and expose verified result. Unknown timeout: query supplier/gateway for actual status before any retry. Background reconciliation must not be silently skipped for financial systems.

## 7. AI architecture and safety contract

1. Gather trip input from authorized user record; redact unnecessary PII.
2. Retrieve only allowed/attributed editorial content and partner listings. Do not use LLM text as provider inventory.
3. Build structured prompt specifying dates, cities, pace, constraints, duration, budget and expected JSON schema.
4. Request from approved AI vendor with timeout, bounded token/cost limits and no secrets exposed.
5. Parse and validate strict schema, dates, time clashes, basic travel feasibility, currency fields and known source limitations.
6. Flag unsourced facts/estimated costs. Reject/repair invalid records instead of inventing confirmations.
7. Store AI-run metadata (provider/model/version, source snapshot references, duration, status, no secret raw prompts or excessive personal data).
8. Accept user edits as the source of truth; maintain versioned trip plan.
9. On service failure return manual editor/template. Budget values always recomputed with the deterministic finance service.

**AI quality tests:** valid five-day plan, no extra dates, poor/unknown locations, budget below plausible minimum, traveler preference changes, cancellation, timeouts, output malformed or context missing. Never silently suggest unavailable tickets or hotels as bookable.

## 8. Relational data contract and migrations

Primary data model is documented in `Backend Schema.md`: identity/profiles/travelers, destinations/places, partners/offers, trips/members, itinerary days/items, budget lines, AI runs, booking intents/verified bookings, payment attempts and provider events, safety contacts, support, notifications and audit. Use MySQL/InnoDB primary/foreign keys; indexed owner/trip lookups; version/concurrency columns; strict states and source timestamps; audit sensitive changes. Migrate schema with MySQL-compatible Flyway migrations and test forward migrations on empty and populated staging DB. Use Java-generated UUID strings in `CHAR(36)` ASCII/binary fields, UTC `DATETIME(6)` and InnoDB `FOREIGN KEY` constraints; avoid PostgreSQL-specific constructs such as `JSONB`, `TIMESTAMPTZ`, `gen_random_uuid()` and partial indexes.

## 9. Security, privacy, consent and compliance

- HTTP TLS enforced, CORS limited to actual origins, secure cookies, CSRF defense for cookie-auth mutating requests, session expiry and revocation.
- Password hashing with approved modern algorithm, credential throttling, verification/reset token single-use or revocation.
- Object-level authorization: users can see only own trips/travelers/bookings; no IDOR via guessed UUID. Admin/support have least privilege and audit.
- Input validation and output encoding; SQL parameterization; safe file upload restrictions only where docs are enabled.
- Optional location data is off by default in V1; Partition 2 consent is explicit/time-limited/revocable and GPS/OS limits are visible.
- No raw payment card data in Ghoomi DB. Processor token/reference only where approved. No government ID collection absent required booking purpose.
- Appropriate India privacy, IT, e-commerce, payment and travel obligations need qualified review before launch; source PDF explicitly marks this as necessary. No template policy alone proves compliance.
- Private logs must not include traveler PII, location histories or payment secrets. Encrypt at rest as available; backups access-controlled and restore-tested.

## 10. Nonfunctional requirements (proposed acceptance targets)

| Attribute | Proposed engineering gate (validate via load/test plan) |
|---|---|
| Availability | Staging monitoring and error budget; production target selected after provider SLA review |
| API latency | Internal CRUD p95 < 500ms under an agreed pilot load; vendor calls separately timed/bounded |
| Provider timeouts | Per-provider configurable bounded timeout + circuit breaker; failure isolated from trip saves |
| Reliability | No duplicate booking/payment for repeated same idempotency key; safe unknown state |
| Data integrity | All trip/budget values persist and respect ownership/version |
| Accessibility | WCAG 2.2 AA-oriented core user flows and keyboard testing |
| Security | Zero known critical/high exploitable auth/financial issues at launch; threat-model review |
| Data recovery | Documented/tested backup restoration; choose RPO/RTO with operator later |
| Cross-device | Chrome/Firefox/Safari responsive smoke tests at mobile/tablet/desktop sizes |
| AI expense | Per-request token/rate limits and instrumentation for model spend; no uncapped retries |

These are **planned targets**, not certified service levels or performance already measured.

## 11. Testing strategy and evidence

- **Unit:** validators, money arithmetic, itinerary conflict detection, status transitions, mode gates.
- **Integration:** migrations, repository queries, auth/session, provider adapter contracts, AI schema parser, webhook signature/idempotency, notification events.
- **E2E:** signup → create 5-day Mumbai/Goa trip → AI/manual plan → edit → budget → save → reopen → provider-verified booking path or truthful restricted state.
- **Security negatives:** user A cannot read/edit user B trip, traveler, ticket, payment, support; admin role restrictions; expired/replayed reset links; malicious inputs.
- **Failure:** supplier timeout, stale quote, AI failure, payment unknown, repeated webhook, DB constraint violation, UI offline reconnect.
- **Release:** staging deployment, regression suite, responsive review, accessibility review, backup restore, rollback exercise, monitoring/alert operator.

## 12. CI/CD, monitoring and operational workflows

Pipeline: PR checks (format/lint/type/build/test/scan) → reviewed merge → staging deploy + migration smoke tests → E2E and provider sandbox tests → release gate → version-tagged production deploy → monitor and rollback on critical regression. Use feature flags for money/provider-dependent actions. Health and metrics endpoints must not expose PII/secrets. Key signals: app errors, signup success, trip save failures, AI failures, provider response quality/latency, quote mismatch, booking unknowns, payment mismatches, notification delivery and support escalation.

## 13. External provider readiness matrix (must be filled from reality)

| Vertical/provider | Approved access | Data contract reviewed | Sandbox tested | Payment/booking rights | Launch mode |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Flights | **Unverified** | No | No | Unknown | `PLANNING_ONLY` until proof |
| Trains | **Unverified** | No | No | Unknown/regulated | `PLANNING_ONLY` until proof |
| Buses | **Unverified** | No | No | Unknown | `PLANNING_ONLY` until proof |
| Cabs | **Unverified** | No | No | Unknown | `PLANNING_ONLY` until proof |
| Hotels | **Unverified** | No | No | Unknown | `PLANNING_ONLY` until proof |
| Maps/places | **Unverified** | No | No | N/A | Curated content until authorized source |
| AI provider | **Unverified** | No | No | N/A | Manual planning fallback until configured |
| Payment gateway | **Unverified** | No | No | Unknown | Disabled until approved |

**Rule:** only change a row to a stronger launch mode with real credentials/rights, tested responses, current terms and recorded evidence; the table's `PLANNING_ONLY` is a safe default, not proof that the category's curated content has already been implemented.

## 14. Future architecture boundaries

Partition 2 introduces verified provider webhooks/settlement workflows, group roles and consent, live update/event ingestion, private documents and partner portal. Partition 3 introduces event-driven disruption graph, approved action orchestration, SOS partners, offline sync, public moderation and advanced financial operations. **Design extendable tables/interfaces now; do not prematurely deploy the future subsystems.**

## 15. Definition of technical done

No feature is done merely because code compiles. It must satisfy mapped `GHO-P1-*` acceptance criteria and relevant `FLOW-*` journeys; include tests with actual results, authorized supplier truth labeling, accessibility, secure storage/authorization, clean migration and tracker evidence. Anything blocked by provider access remains `Blocked` or `Planning-only`, never `Implemented/direct booking`.


========== \UI-UX design Brief.md ==========
# GHOOMI — UI/UX Design Brief

**Revision:** 1.0 • **Active stage:** Partition 1, before first deployment • **Source:** 53-page master PDF sections 14–40 and 91. **Visual palette and layout choices are design recommendations, not finalized brand assets.**

## 1. Vision, audience and design principles

Ghoomi should feel like a reliable modern travel marketplace, but its signature experience must be **complete-trip planning**. Brand line: *You Enjoy the Journey. Ghoomi Manages the Journey.* Vision: *From Home to Home.* Design for first-time travelers, students, solo travelers, friends, couples, families and support/admin operators.

- **Search first, plan deeply.** Expose familiar flight/train/bus/cab/hotel tabs and a prominent `Plan my trip with AI` action.
- **Clarity beats cleverness.** Users must understand whether they are planning, viewing a supplier quote, going to an external provider, or actually booking.
- **One trip context.** Carry origin, destination, dates, travelers, selection and estimated budget from discovery to itinerary to dashboard.
- **User agency.** Edit AI suggestions, skip transport/stay when already arranged, control cost level and undo when supported.
- **Trust through content.** Display source, freshness, currency, amount basis, fees/cancellation conditions where verified. Never invent badges, scarcity, reviews or booking confirmations.
- **Accessibility and speed.** Semantic HTML, visible focus, keyboard-friendly controls, responsive composition, reduced motion, optimized licensed imagery.

## 2. Proposed visual system

The product source does not specify an approved palette. These are starter tokens to keep coding-agent screens visually consistent until the user confirms the branding.

| Token | Proposed value | Intended use |
|---|---|---|
| Navy | `#102B46` | Navigation, hero overlay, strong headings |
| Teal | `#087F8C` | Primary button, selected nav and links |
| Teal hover | `#056571` | Interactive hover/pressed states |
| Coral | `#F97355` | Small accents (never sole meaning) |
| Canvas | `#F7FAFC` | Light page background |
| Surface | `#FFFFFF` | Cards, dialogs, forms |
| Text | `#193247` | Normal body text |
| Muted | `#597184` | Metadata with sufficient contrast |
| Border | `#DAE5EB` | Card separators and inputs |
| Success | `#147D54` | Verified status only |
| Warning | `#9B5B10` | Estimated, changed or unverified data |
| Error | `#B93232` | Failure / destructive actions |
| Focus | `#176FCE` | Focus ring |
| Radius | 10px controls / 16px cards | Consistent surface shape |

Typography proposal: Inter or system UI font, 16px body / 1.5 line-height, 14px metadata, 30–44px heading depending on width. Use 8px spacing rhythm, max content width around 1280px and subtle card shadows. Photos must be licensed/relevant and not used as false proof of hotel/service inventory. Color alone never communicates verification.

### 2.1 Responsive and accessibility contracts

| Viewport | Layout guidance |
|---|---|
| 320–599px mobile | Single-column forms/results, prominent primary CTA, scrollable travel tabs, compact progress stepper, mobile-safe filter drawer |
| 600–1023px tablet | Flexible 2-column cards, wrapping search fields, summary moved below planner content when necessary |
| >=1024px desktop | 12-column grid, top global nav, results with filter sidebar, planning workspace with optional budget sidebar |

Target WCAG 2.2 AA, meaningful input labels, error summaries, keyboard-accessible tabs/reordering, polite announcements for result count/budget changes, adequate contrast, reasonable touch targets, skip link and focus retention when dialogs open/close. Don't hide important status in tooltips only.

## 3. Information architecture and navigation

**Public:** `Flights`, `Trains`, `Buses`, `Cabs`, `Hotels`, `Holidays`, `Explore`, `Login`, `Sign Up`. Food and activities may live in Explore/More. **Signed-in:** `Dashboard`, `My Trips`, `Plan a Trip`, `My Bookings` (only with truthful mode), `Notifications`, `Safety`, `Support`, `Profile`. **Admin:** separate role-gated navigation.

Maintain a consistent search form pattern: category → criteria → loading/results → compare/detail → add to trip or supported provider checkout. Maintain persistent `Save draft` and a breadcrumb/stepper in the trip wizard. No dead CTA to unimplemented future pages.

## 4. Reusable UI components and rules

| Component | Required content and interaction |
|---|---|
| `TravelModeTabs` | Icon+label flight/train/bus/cab/stay/holiday tabs; keyboard support and correct fields per tab |
| `SearchForm` | Origin/destination/place autocomplete; dates; travelers; clear validation; preserves inputs on errors |
| `DestinationCard` | Licensed image, place name, short travel context, link to guide or create trip |
| `OfferCard` | Provider name, duration/timing, taxes/fees/price basis, source+freshness, capability mode and next action |
| `SourceBadge` | `Live verified`, `Provider listed`, `Estimate`, `Unknown/Unavailable` with explanatory legend |
| `PriceBreakdown` | Supplier amount, applicable taxes and platform fees, discount if genuine, total, currency, per-person/total |
| `TripStepper` | Progress, back/next/save-exit, current step and descriptive headings |
| `DayCard` | Day/date, activities in order, times, estimated durations, costs and accessible reorder/edit/remove actions |
| `TripBudgetPanel` | Chosen budget, estimated sum, remaining, category breakdown, assumption footnotes |
| `BookingStatusChip` | `External/unverified`, `Pending`, `Confirmed`, `Failed`, `Unknown` with source/time |
| `SafetyCard` | Contacts + official emergency information, no guarantee of rescue or live monitoring |
| `NotificationsList` | Real events, status, link to relevant trip/booking/support ticket |
| `Error/Empty/Loading` | Explicit source-dependent errors, safe retries, helpful next action; no fake fallback inventory |

## 5. Partition 1 page specifications, aligned to master inventory

### 5.1 Public landing page (page 1)

- **Above the fold:** scenic travel imagery with readable overlay, Ghoomi brand, core search tabs and a separate `Plan a Trip with AI` CTA.
- **Main body:** popular destination guides, holiday ideas, benefits of unified planning (editable itinerary, estimated spend, saved trips), supported transport/stay categories and service policies.
- **Footer:** support, contact, privacy/terms, booking responsibility disclosures. Use no invented partner logos, testimonials or offers.
- **Suggested hero:** “One journey. One place to plan it.” Supporting copy: “Explore travel options, create a personalized itinerary and keep your budget organized.”

### 5.2 Identity and user dashboard (pages 2–7)

- Sign Up/Login: clean centered form, strong labels, password visibility, reasonable validation, subtle illustration at larger widths; display async/auth errors without account enumeration.
- Verification/forgot password: expiry, retry limits, safe resending and next steps.
- Profile: basic fields, travel preferences, saved travelers and emergency contacts, clearly optional sensitive fields.
- Signed-in overview: quick `Create trip`, draft/recent trip cards, notifications and truthful provider-service availability.

### 5.3 Destination search and details (pages 8–9)

Search by destination and themes (culture, budget, weekend, adventure). Detail view contains verified/curated overview, photos, attractions, hotels, food, activities and helpful travel tips. Local events/opening hours require source and freshness or `Not verified`. The `Add to Trip` action selects a saved trip or starts a new one.

### 5.4 Trip creation / traveler details / AI planning (pages 10–12)

Recommended wizard: `Route & dates → Travelers → Interests & comfort → Budget & plan mode → Generate → Customize & save`. Each stage validates and persists after explicit save or confirmed autosave. Modes: **Budget, Balanced, Comfort, Custom**; category overrides for transport, stays, meals and activities. `Own Vehicle` and `Already Have Stay` skip unwanted booking.

### 5.5 AI itinerary and customization (pages 13, 15)

Desktop: day tabs at left, editable chronological timeline center, budget summary right. Mobile: day selector and vertically stacked cards with expandable summary. Actions: add/remove place, edit time, reorder, change category, regenerate selected day with scope warning, undo when supported, save. Distinguish suggested vs verified availability and cite any supplier data behind price/time claims. AI failures show `Continue manually` and restore existing plan.

### 5.6 Budget estimate (page 14)

Top summary: `Budget`, `Estimated trip cost`, `Difference`, `Estimated per person` only when mathematically valid. Categories: outbound/return transport, accommodation, meals, local travel, activities, taxes/fees where supported, emergency reserve, other. Changes must recompute real deterministic amounts, not just switch cosmetic chart figures. Show `Estimated for planning, recheck supplier costs before booking`.

### 5.7 My Trips and trip hub (pages 16–17)

My Trips: saved draft/planned/active/completed cards with route, dates, type, budget, action `Continue planning`. Trip Hub: identity of trip, time/date summary, itinerary, selected transport/stay, budget panel, booking status, basic safety and support. Suggested tabs `Overview | Itinerary | Transport | Stay | Budget | Bookings | Safety`. Selected planned items are not assumed booked. No animated live traveler marker in initial launch.

### 5.8 Transport (pages 18–31)

Shared search-result system with header criteria, comparison/filters, source badges and detail page. Vertical-specific details:

- **Flights:** carrier, departure/arrival, stop count, luggage when supplied, terms, total vs per-person fare, supplier timestamp.
- **Trains:** exact stations, schedule, class, seat status only if authorized; booking handoff clearly states supplier/rail authority.
- **Buses:** operator, class, boarding/drop points, date, route, price and supplier terms.
- **Cabs:** pickup/drop, service type (local/airport/outstation), car category, estimated or partner quote and provider responsibility.
- **Own vehicle:** route-planning guidance and user-adjustable estimated fuel/tolls; no checkout.

Each results page requires no-results, unavailable-provider, invalid-criteria and quote-stale states. Filtering/sorting must use underlying record values, not ornamental controls.

### 5.9 Hotels / stays (pages 32–38)

Search by destination/check-in/check-out/guests/rooms; result grid/list with rating source, property images, amenities, location, rate provenance and cancellation terms when verified. Detail page has gallery, room details, guest form only when needed, cost summary and booking capability. If not authorized for direct booking, `Continue at provider` or `Add to trip`; never simulated confirmed booking. Provide `Already have accommodation` mode.

### 5.10 Food and attractions (pages 39–44)

Restaurant and activity lists use location, cuisine/category, approximate expense, photos and reviews only when sourced. Include detail pages with map or route context if licensed mapping exists, and `Add to itinerary`. No implicit food delivery or ticketing when discovery-only.

### 5.11 Booking and payment operations (pages 45–50)

For **authorized direct** checkout: concise stepper `Review → Revalidate → Traveler details → Payment provider → Pending verification → Supplier-confirmed result`. Show total breakdown, explicit terms and separate provider/payment status. On quote change require fresh approval. External redirect clearly discloses provider responsibility. Unknown payment status uses `We're checking your transaction` and a support path, never optimistic success.

### 5.12 Safety, notifications, support, admin (pages 51–55)

- Safety: maintain emergency contacts and source-verified emergency information; no automated SOS dispatch.
- Notifications: read/unread message with event type/status and accurate timestamp.
- Support: support request type, trip/booking association, description, created ticket ID and tracked status.
- Admin: clean role-gated tables for users, destinations, providers, reports and tickets; avoid exposing sensitive PII; require confirmation on destructive actions.
- Partner management in V1: **admin-side integration records only**; full self-service partner interface moves to Partition 2.

## 6. Required microcopy and truth-state labels

| Situation | Use | Do not use |
|---|---|---|
| AI plan | `Suggested itinerary — review before travel` | `Everything is booked` |
| Estimate | `Estimated total (subject to supplier price)` | `Guaranteed final cost` |
| No supplier API | `Live booking not available here` | `Book now` leading to simulated success |
| External checkout | `Continue to [provider] to complete booking` | `Booked with Ghoomi` |
| Unverified external reference | `Reference added, not verified` | `Confirmed` |
| Payment status unknown | `Payment status being verified` | `Successful` without gateway evidence |
| AI failure | `AI plan unavailable. Continue planning manually.` | Empty/false generated plan |
| Emergency info | `Contact official emergency services directly` | `Help has been dispatched` without verified integration |

## 7. State design and design QA

Every page must have: **initial, submitting/loading, populated, no-results, validation error, provider outage (where applicable), auth/permission failure, and success**. Crucial data changes (budget, booking status, saved trip) announce result clearly; errors cannot be toast-only. Use real backend DTOs and explicit source freshness; do not confuse cached places with real-time service availability.

Minimum design handoff: home, signup/login, service search/results/details, trip wizard, AI itinerary/editor, budget, dashboard, supplier redirect or authorized checkout, booking status, safety and support at **desktop ~1440px**, **tablet ~768px** and **mobile ~390px**. Verify keyboard path, focus, form validation, currency formatting and accessible reordering. Future pages reuse established shell/components, not a separate inconsistent visual system.

## 8. Future visual expansion (not active build)

- **Partition 2:** group invitations/member permissions, family monitoring with explicit location grant/revoke, synchronized trip timeline, cancellation/refund status, protected booking documents, partner support and advanced operations UI.
- **Partition 3:** disruptions and alternate itineraries with approval and cost delta, Back-to-Home, SOS with delivery/source truth, travel assistant, verified events/weather, expense splitting, offline read-only caches, Ghoomi Explore, privacy-conscious public trips, community moderation, marketplace and premium plans.

For exact source page inventory and route mapping see `App Flow.md`; for UI acceptance criteria use `prd.md`; for implementation ordering see `Implementation plan.md`.
